# elysium-bot-CHANGELOG.md — bot / arena / cardfx release notes, newest first (started 9 Sep 2026)

*Convention: `BOT_VERSION` carries only the current release note; at each bump the outgoing note is appended here. History before this file: `elysium-history-2026-09-09.md` (main project's archive zip).*

## Rounds without a release
- **Toreador at the sim table (6 Nov 2026, bot v0.6.217, no bot change; PR #52)** — STATUS Next 1, Johan's go of 6 Nov: does the
  Toreador precon hold up beside the starters (D-98)? `docs/reports/REPORT-toreador-sim-2026-11-06.md`. The specified design (the four
  starters + toreador, every seating × 2 deals = 240 at five seats, the client profile; the prediction registered and pushed before the
  run, `e2bc196`): Toreador 0.60 VP/match, 11.2 % wins, inside the span. The egress review found that its deals repeat — a deck's deal
  is fixed by its seat and the deal seed, so each deck met 10 deals — which doubles the SEs; a 600-match series with a fresh deal per
  match (`--bench none`) carries the read: Toreador 0.65 ± 0.05 VP/match, 13.0 % wins, level with tremere (−0.01 ± 0.07 VP paired),
  above nosferatu (+0.26 ± 0.06); the starters keep their order. Every Toreador card played; politics land (99 % of its referendums
  pass); the Grand Ball burned before it pays (325 played, 304 burned, 0.75 bars each, never before a bleed). Audits as on the
  four-seat design; the three `strike-taken` flags show the stray combat row's cause (a failed blocker fights the later blocker's
  combat, and can spend a strike card). **`ana-cards.py`**: its untitled list read 135 false plays (bare precon crypt names missed the
  `(G6)` keys; a title card taken earlier, R-43) — it now resolves through the alias and honours a `title` row (a planted pair is
  listed, the real list is empty). HOWTO: a deck read takes fresh deals. BOARD: a held master counts as played. LESSONS L-84 (a
  stacked PR targets `main`, after #49). Egress: 4 findings (unproven 3, half-done 1), all fixed.
- **C1 — the docs diet (20 Oct 2026, no code change; PR #39)** — `spec-cleanup-2026-10-20.md` §5: the prescribed bootstrap of ~700 KB became
  root CLAUDE.md + `bot/CLAUDE.md` (rules only) + `docs/STATUS.md` + `docs/BOARD.md` (~35 KB), with `RULINGS.md` (the register of
  Johan's rulings and the design decisions, R / D / O numbered), `LESSONS.md` (the learnings distilled), `ARCHITECTURE.md`,
  `HARNESS.md` (the round, the egress review, the release ritual) and `HOWTO.md` (from SKILLS.md) read when a task touches them.
  The old CLAUDE.md (handshake and debts 1–71), the journal, the context, the learnings, SKILLS.md, every READ-ME-FIRST, the
  consumed handoffs and the delivered round specs moved verbatim to `docs/history/` (the extraction checklist beside them).
  Debts 64–65 became `specs/spec-knowledge-layer.md`. `tools/doc-budget.sh` checks the budgets, the named paths and the version.
  The release ritual is now STATUS + CHANGELOG + the PR body (no READ-ME-FIRST, no journal entry); the project instruction is
  `docs/PROJECT-INSTRUCTIONS.md` (the spec's Appendix A). After review: the root CLAUDE.md's bot pointers; the two Claude Docs of
  25 Sep copied into `specs/` with a status table (`spec-event-log-design-2026-09-25.md`, `spec-cardfx-sweep-sources-2026-09-25.md`);
  the old handshake one release per line (`history/handshake-v0.6.112-v0.6.211.md`); the VTES-Player review moved to Johan's
  private project notes, its four open items credited on the BOARD; LESSONS numbered L-1..81 and cited from CLAUDE.md;
  `doc-budget.sh` portable to bash 3.2 and checking every version STATUS names. Gates on the branch: suite 2244/0, canary 69/0
  (bot/'s copies and the root's release), HB1 11/11, hotseat 11/0.

## Bot
- **v0.6.226 (14 Nov 2026)** — Round D, RULINGS O-40 → D-115 (Johan, 14 Nov 12:06: "vi gör på ditt förslag i O-40"): Smiling
  Jack's self-consistent runway, the knob `jackRunway` (default 1 after a no-loss bench; 0 = v0.6.225), spec §2's amendment.
  - *The change* (`_jackPriceAt`): a seat's runway against Jack R solves R·(c₁ + fc) + R(R−1)/2 = pool, the positive root
    √(b² + 2·pool) − b with b = c₁ + fc − ½; H = min(R, Jack's life), so the price over R is pool − R·fc, never above the pool
    (v0.6.225's flat pool / (c₁ + fc) let it pass: 5 of 56 burns on the 240). A runway of a round or more is never longer than the
    flat one, R = 1 exactly at pool = c₁ + fc (the coordination's "a round" stays); the owner's life (a flat 1 + fc a round) is
    unchanged. Every Jack read takes it: my price, my prey's, the bots shorter than me, `wDef` (my runway against my turns to oust my
    prey) and my own Jack's worth. Section 171 (4/0; 0/4 on v0.6.225): R = 7.26 and a price of exactly my 30 pool at c₁ 1 (the flat
    runway: 30 rounds, 465); no price above its pool over 1200 reads (240 above it at 0); 169b's table at Nils's 30 — Hanna's Jack
    takes 3 + 4 + 5 from my 10 pool before I can oust Nils, the flat runway priced that 13.89 and Nils's 27, the consistent one 10 and
    16.6, and my vampire now burns Jack instead of bleeding (4.04 against Enchant Kindred's 3.25); my Jack's worth at most offW times
    my prey's pool. Section 169's fixture keeps the flat runway (its figures are its prices). Arena v1.4.12 whitelists the knob.
  - *Predictions* (registered before any measurement, D-93, commit d56e349, word for word; v0.6.225's figures from its own traces,
    the 240 and the 600):
    (K0) at 0: IDENTICAL to v0.6.225 on the 240, the V5 smoke and the 600 (the version string set back; the manifest's
    `"jackRunway":0` dropped, the only expected difference). At 1, every seat, against v0.6.225: (R1) a match without a Smiling Jack
    in play is IDENTICAL; each DIVERGED match's first difference comes once a Jack is in play; `ana-gates.py` PASS on all three
    tables. (R2) no plan row prices Jack above its seat's pool (v0.6.225: 70 of 331 rows on the 240, 335 of 1366 on the 600) and
    none at 100 or more (28, 98); no burn candidate or pick priced above the actor's pool at the decision (candidates 5 and 44,
    picks 5 and 29), save a pool spent after the minion read (each such case listed with its cause). (R3) the defence's worth
    (`jackOwn`) at most 60 — offW ≤ 1.5 times a prey's pool — on the 240, the 600 and the bench (377 now); the 240's block-as-target
    worth likewise (378). (R4) the burn picks within a quarter of v0.6.225's: the 240 42–70 (56), the 600 161–269 (215) — the prices
    fall where the runway bound H, little at the late short runways where the two runways nearly agree, and wDef 2 comes more often
    (a shorter runway passes under my turns to oust my prey): its share of candidates at least 80 % on the 240 (80 %), 77 % on the
    600 (77 %). (R5) the tenfold picks (a burn worth ten times its alternative) do not rise (12, 37), none priced above the actor's
    pool (5, 17). (R6) Jack's median life 1–2 rounds on the 240 (1.0), 1–3 on the 600 (2.0); Nosferatu's paired VP against no
    burns (v0.6.224's K0 traces) still below 0 on the 240 and the 600 (−0.13, −0.10), the runway's own move on it (v0.6.225 → this)
    within ±0.10. (R7) the mirror bench (the four starters, every seat the variant in turn, 288; every seat at 0, the variant at
    1 — the burn is on at every seat, so the owner's and the burner's reads both fire there, L-91 does not bite): no loss, the
    variant seat's 95 % interval reaches 0 or above; `ana-gates.py` on its arms fails only the ledger gate every fresh-deal bench
    fails (BOARD). (F) if (R7) holds, `jackRunway` flips to 1 in this PR (D-114's rule) and (R1)–(R6) are read on the release bytes.
  - *What came out* (the release bytes at 1, the version set back, against v0.6.225; the knob's label normalized in the manifests):
    (K0) held — IDENTICAL 240, the V5 smoke 168 and the 600 (4800 manifest rows normalized). (R1) held — the 240 DIVERGED 9,

    DIVERGED 3), Jack burned 47 times in each arm, the variant's own worth reads at most 14.9; `ana-gates.py` on both arms fails only
    the ledger gate, the three known faults (BOARD). A small footprint: the change moves a decision only where a Jack's price was
    near its alternative.
  - *The flip* (F): `jackRunway` default 1 in this PR.
  - *Found on the way:* my Jack's worth to me (spec §5) sets my prey's loss over her runway — since D-115 at most her pool —

    20 of 36 on the 240 (22 of 37) and 97 of 152 on the 600 (107 of 165). A design question for Johan (RULINGS O-41), not this
    round's to change.
- **v0.6.225 (14 Nov 2026)** — Round C, RULINGS D-113 (3)–(4) (Johan, 13 Nov, and his order of 21:45): a human's Toreador Grand
  Ball read first and asked last, and the cardfx `memo` descriptor, `specs/spec-human-ball-memo-2026-11-14.md`.
  - *The descriptor* (4): Toreador Grand Ball's curated entry carries `memo` — what the card picks (two Toreador), its lasting roles
    (free, lock: the bot's own field, the token on the vampire, the token on the card, how a human's choice is read) and the
    question. `_memoMap` reads its texts (v0.6.221's, in their order: 170f pins them; 170g, a changed text is the one written).
  - *A human's Ball* (3; `_hMemoLog`, `_hMemoResolve`, `_hMemoChat`): every bot records the play line's acting minion and target;
    at the end of the owner's master phase it reads the one of their Toreador that turned locked, then the free one — the pair
    less the locked one, a custom token naming the card, their only other candidate; still open, the bot that speaks for the
    table asks once in chat (`NAME, your Toreador Grand Ball: which Toreador acts unblocked — A or B?`), and the owner's answer
    naming one candidate is every bot's memory. `_humanBallFree`: a Ball whose free Toreador is known frees that one alone; one
    still unknown keeps the generous rule. The records live in the table model (`this.opp[seat].memo`, reset at every deal). The
    readings go to the bot's own log (`memo: …`), never a table line; a `memo` trace row records each. The player guide's "Where its
    understanding ends" names the question. The undirected lines a human's client may suffix with a leftover target (a hunt, an
    own-vampire rescue, leave torpor, an undirected card action) are read with it. Section 170: 16/0; 10/6 on the candidate the
    review read (170k, l, n, o, p, q), 1/15 on v0.6.224's bytes (170f, the identity pin, green on both by design).
  - *The egress review* (fresh context, 12 findings: half-done 3, rule-missed 2, test-weakened 2, unproven 3, stale-doc 2; 12 fixed):
    the records were never reset between games (a rematch's card ids start again at c1, so game 2's Ball could inherit game 1's free
    one) — now in the table model; the descriptor's `pick.n` and the lock role's `read` were written, never read — now read; the
    token read took any token naming the card, the lock's own text and a token left from before the play line included — now only a
    new one, not another role's; the fixture stubbed the bot's log lines (170j now captures them); 170a's actor was also the free one
    (170h, 170i); the readings at an action line and at the turn's end, two Balls and the board's 600 ms lag were untested — the
    lag also read the lock too early, now read on their next board (170m–o); the answer window, corrections and accents (170p);
    "neither misled the bots" was wrong — the leftover target hid a hunt from every bot (170q, the handoff's ask); the evidence's
    chat lines; the guide's condition ("and", not "or"; tokens), the spec's reading paths and end, the D-113 quote, D-101's list.

- **v0.6.224 (14 Nov 2026)** — Round B, RULINGS D-114 (Johan's "kör", 13 Nov 20:13 and 21:45): Smiling Jack's burn weighed by its
  price, `specs/spec-jack-burn-2026-11-13.md`. Two steps, each with its own footprint, on round A (v0.6.223).
  - *Step 1, the block guard* (a rules correction, no knob; section 168: 3/0, 0/3 on v0.6.223): the threat classes' block path
    read every window as undirected (+1 stealth), so a bare body never blocked a rush or an action to burn my card — both directed
    (R-12). At stealth 0 it now runs the bleed path's bare-block tests (a body unlocked or behind a wake; `bareBlock` off declines,
    chump only with a chump body against a combat threat), then weighs the block as any: a disabling deck's rush block scores
    1.31, a resisting deck's 0.23.
  - *Step 2, the Jack burn* (the knob `jackBurn`; section 169: 6/0; 0/6 on v0.6.223, 3/3 on the review's bytes, 5/1 on the first fix's):
    Smiling Jack's cardfx gains "Vampires can burn this card as a (D) action" (`burnCardAction`, `burnBy`). `_planJack` writes
    `plan.reads.jack` at each strategize (the plan rule; its line in `specs/elysium-bot-strategy.md`): what Jack takes from me over
    H = min(my runway, Jack's life) rounds, H·c₁ + H(H−1)/2 with c₁ the counter at my next unlock, my prey's price, the bots with
    a shorter runway and this turn's wait. `_bestBurnCardFor` reaches Jack at any seat with P(success) × [wDef · my saving − offW
    · my prey's saving] × aggression (wDef 2 when my runway is shorter than my turns to oust my prey); the bot with the shortest
    runway burns first, the others wait one round, once per Jack; a burn at my own Jack is weighed by its worth
    (`plan.reads.jackOwn`: what it still takes from my prey, less my own payments). `ana-jack.py` v1.0.0 (new, read-only: Jack's
    plays, how each left play, its rounds in play, the knob's burn picks); arena v1.4.11 whitelists the knob; the coverage tool
    v1.1.46 classifies `burnBy`; the ledger and the Nosferatu starter's coverage report regenerated.
  - *The egress reviews* (fresh context). The first, 11 findings: fixed — H floored (a seat under a round of runway paid 0); c₁
    one too many for a seat that unlocks before the owner (my prey, in the turn order from the seat acting now); the Jack reads
    computed in the tactics (now plan reads); the wait written by a read and never reset; `burnBy` written, never read; section 168
    asserting only a why text (168b, 168c: a block, through the real window); four stale pages (ARCHITECTURE's burn row, the
    ledger and the coverage report, the defence's wording, the 0-path's label). Measured — the owner's defence, which a mirror
    bench cannot fire (its owner and its burners never both carry the knob there; LESSONS L-91): a bench pinned to Nosferatu,
    below. Listed (BOARD) — the chump gate is not the commit (both block paths); three ledger faults on fresh deals. The second,
    read-only, of the fixes: 10 findings (half-done 1, test-weakened 3, stale-doc 4, unproven 2), all fixed — the coordination
    wait was fixed at the unlock read, before Jack's payments (a minion read now re-decides it in the deferring turn: against the
    first fix the 240 DIVERGED 3, the V5 smoke 2, the 600 9); 169b, 169c and 169f proved c₁, the defer's turn and the `burnBy`
    read only indirectly (now directly: 169 is 5/1 on the first fix's bytes, 3/3 on the review's, 0/6 on v0.6.223; LESSONS L-90);
    the strategy line, ARCHITECTURE's row, the knob comment and the ledger; J′'s first explanation (prices that never reach a
    decision) and K0′'s V5 evidence (re-diffed: IDENTICAL).
  - *Predictions* (registered before each footprint): (G1) half held — the 240 changed 40 matches (DIVERGED 10, SAME-PLAY 30): the

    step 1, the knob's label normalized in the manifest and — not foreseen — in the sim's result row (`--knob jackBurn=0` labels
    every seat there; the first read was SAME-PLAY on every match for that row alone). (J′) the 240's burn picks rose to 56, beyond

    unblockable', the locked one 'Grand Ball: does not unlock', the Ball 'Unblockable: NAME' and 'Locked: NAME' (two tokens: two long

    last-resort seed (no Node `crypto`, no Web Crypto) mixes a per-load counter: the float product `Date.now() * 2654435761` left
    its low 19 bits 0, and two bots made in one millisecond shared a seed.

  - *Before range*: Torn Signpost (the base strength this combat), Form of the Wolf and Monstrous Form (+1 strength), Rage of
    Apedemak (+N on hand strikes) and Hunger of Marduk (this round's ranged steal; no card leaves the hand at the strike) are played
    by the look-ahead rule — when `_strikePick` deals more with the card at close, or at long for a snipe holding the maneuver
    (`decide('before-range')`; a range already set for the round is weighed alone, `fixed`), one card at a time. Form of the Wolf
    [PRO]'s optional press is this round's (`_bundledPress`). A log line per card, never a `plays` line; the round row's `br`.
  - *The maneuvers go back and forth between bots*: the answering bot can answer a maneuver to long with one to close (the chase
    posture); the acting bot answers that while it wants long and holds another maneuver; the acting bot says nothing more when it
    lets the range stand, so the answering bot meets a late counter in its before-strikes wait and answers it (`does not maneuver.`
    or a counter). Every counter is under the combat mind (combatMind 0 = v0.6.213). No new line shape; a round without a counter
    sends the old lines. Against a human the range step is unchanged (BOARD). Rows: `counter`, `commit`.
  - *Slam [POT]*'s maneuver to close (no plain maneuver left) commits the round's strike: played and paid in the range step, no dodge

    round (committed to Slam by its maneuver; long range).`). No strike card's or weapon's maneuver once the round's strike is
    chosen (the rulebook, combat step 2: two Slams, `_strikeBound`). The no-strike line (v0.6.174's, for a gun under Immortal
    Grapple) is now READ by the other bot as the strike step's answer — it waited out its safety net and took the rulebook's 1.

  - *(2) EQUIPMENT* (Johan: "they should weigh more than 1-bleeds without modifiers at least — Govern is very often better; the Magnum is stable except at the end of the game when you are low on pool"): a weapon or an armour (equip tier 2–3) beats a bare plain bleed of 1 — no crypt bonus, no bleed modifier in hand (`ctx.plainBare`). It never beats a bleed card, never with the prey within reach (the lunge rule), and a pool-cost item at 6 pool or less keeps its flat price (`ctx.lowPool`). Knob `equipOverPlain`, 0 = the flat 1.15 × economy weight.
  - *(3) SPYING MISSION [OBF]* (Johan, option B: "that is why you have the card in the deck"): a bounced bleed of 2 or more about to land on somebody other than my prey is made unsuccessful — no pool, no Edge, the frozen line `L12.spyNull`, and the target's pending goes to 0. The card goes on the vampire with +2 banked against that Methuselah, burned the next time it is about to successfully bleed them (a modifier's shape: the L12.add line, then a reactions window). Knob `spyCancelMin` 2, 0 = never. At equal stealth the card is spent last (Johan: "you play it as stealth last of the stealth cards you already have in hand"); a combat dual card (Swallowed by the Night) is still kept over it.

- **v0.6.210 (19 Oct 2026)** — found by the sim table while v0.6.209's numbers were checked (the round's own review; Johan's 16 Oct combining rule, as a PR of its own stacked on #35). *THE LAST POOL:* `_bestMasterFor` admitted a master at `cost > pool`, so a bot at pool 1 paid its last pool for Vessel (or Wider View, Ventrue Headquarters, The Labyrinth) and one at pool 2 for a Hunting Ground (or Arcane Library, Pentex Subversion, Warsaw Station, Guardian Angel): 35 bots in 33 of the 240 baseline matches on v0.6.209 (Tremere 12, Nosferatu 10, Ventrue 9, Malkavian 4), every one in its own master phase. "A Methuselah with no pool is ousted", but nothing ousted the bot: it played on at 0 — 101 actions through, 65 card plays, 46 blocks and 5 referendums called after the payment, and 3 of the 35 went on to win — until a bleed took it below 0, while the other bots' no-server net (v0.6.147) had already read it as out. Now a pool cost leaves me ≥ 1 pool in the master finder and the master loop's re-check, the equip finder (.44 Magnum costs 2) and the fetch finder's target; the ally finder's "leave >= 1 pool" and the political finder's `>=` were already right. The net behind them is the turn engine (the v0.6.140 / v0.6.202 audit, one phase further): 0 pool after my master phase, or before a minion's action, ousts me at once, and a master-phase oust stops the turn as an unlock-phase one does. Footprint (`ana-sim-diff.py`, v0.6.209 → v0.6.210, the same 240-match design): 200 SAME-PLAY, 40 DIVERGED, every one first at a master pick — the 33 matches with a bot at 0 pool (23 of them with another outcome), and 7 where a Misdirection at pool 1 was picked and then held for want of a target (nothing paid; with that one row dropped the matches are identical). Master picks costing the last pool 44 → 0; bots deciding at 0 pool 35 → 0; the net never fired. Section 156 (8; 2/6 on v0.6.209 — 156a, the first 156c, 156d–g red); 19c's "with 1 pool both income assets are affordable" was the bug and now reads pool 2; section 24's live Parthenon integration played a 2-pool card at pool 2 (the first suite run's five reds) and now sets pool 3.

With two Methuselahs left the only side seat is the caller, so a 2-player KRC at prey pool 1 burned 3 of the bot's own pool (prey −1, me −3), at prey pool 2 it split 2 + 2. Two of the first 24 rotated sim matches ended with nobody standing; in the first, KRC took Ventrue from 6 to 3, Daring the Dawn's damage sent its famed Madison to torpor and Fame burned the rest, after its last opponent was already out. Now the overflow goes to a side seat that is not me and stays on the prey when there is none (prey −3, me −1); with three or more Methuselahs v0.6.126 stands. *THE SELF-OUSTING REFERENDUM* (the same class one step further: the second of those two matches, and match 175 of the first 240-match baseline): `_bestPoliticalFor` weighed my own pool at 0.8 and added 3 for my prey's oust, so a 2-player Ancilla Empowerment at pool 4 with four minions (me −4, prey −4) scored +3.8 and ousted both; its cost guard only looked at the card's cost. Now a referendum whose passing ousts me (its pool cost plus my share of the terms ≥ my pool) is never called. Footprint on the same 240-match design (`ana-sim-diff.py`, v0.6.208 → v0.6.209): 16 matches changed play, every one at a Ventrue referendum (10 KRC splits moved off the caller; 6 calls the guard dropped: 2 KRC, 4 Ancilla), 3 changed outcome, post-game ousts 1 → 0. Before, the `[^a-z0-9]` strip deleted them: inside the bot that was consistent (`normIdx` and the lookup lost the same letter, so the printed spelling hit), but a deck typing the plain-letter spelling — *Przemyslaw, War Beast*, *Boleslaw Gutowski*, *Clara Hjortshoj*, *Sacre-Coeur Cathedral, France* — missed all three tiers of `fxLookup`: capacity 3 (the fallback), no clan, no fx, never played, while the client finds those cards since v2.6.167. `prepCardfx` rebuilds `normIdx` at load, so `elysium-cardfx.json` is unchanged. - **v0.6.207 (17 Oct 2026)** — Johan's two answers on v0.6.206's proposals (17 Oct: "Vi kan gå på dina rekommendationer för 1 och 2"), given after PR #26 was merged — so a release of their own, not a change to v0.6.206. The analysis and the measurements are `docs/specs/spec-next-2026-10-17.md` §2–§3. *(1) THE PROBE DEFAULT:* `probeHold` **2** — the probers keep their hands off the heavy card only when my actors out-number my prey's unlocked bodies at the minion phase's start (Johan's bait read). The probe lab (v0.6.206's `lab-probe.js`, 360 tables, the same table in every arm): +0.80 ± 0.14 pool per probe turn when I out-number (the Govern through 56 times instead of 8 in 91 tables; better on 43, worse on 5), 0.00 otherwise. `--knob probeHold=0` is the rollback (as v0.6.143–206 played: the order, no reservation). Johan's note on the five worse tables — all a prey with one unlocked body and a wake in hand — is a knowledge-layer item: does the deck hold wakes at all, and how likely is one in hand now, given the cards it has cycled and the wakes it has played (`bot/CLAUDE.md` 65f; not built). The guide's 'Posture' now says it: when a stealth-bleed bot has more vampires ready than you have blockers up, it probes with chump bleeds and keeps the big Govern for the last one. *(2) THE CHAT BEAT (main's finding 1, REPLY v0.6.204 §4.1 — proposal A, main's first idea, Johan's "kör"):* after a chat line `_drain` made the next message, whatever it was, wait the chat beat (4.96 s at the client's 3.1 s pace) — the verdict line, a counter write, the table button's line, a quick phrase, the hold and resume lines. Now only a CHAT waits it, measured from the last chat on the wire (`_lastChatWireAt`; `say()` keeps stamping `_lastChatAt` at queue time for `_pace`); everything else rides the 200 ms cascade, and a hold or resume line (`_holdTags`) skips the beat — it answers the human's own Hold on. At the client's pace the verdict comes at 3.04 s (was 4.96 on a 3 s clock), the table button's line, a typed `+1` and a quick phrase at 0.20 s (4.96), the hold line at 1.60 s (4.96) and the resume line at 6.00 s; two chat bubbles stay 4.96 s apart. The bot's own acts still wait out the bubble in `_pace` (Johan's v0.6.100 rule), one queue keeps the order, and pace 0 (the arena) is untouched. *Tests:* 151d (the manifest without the option reads 2) and 151e (the default plays as 2 through the minion phase; 151a runs 0 explicitly), section 153 (4: the analysis' scenarios on the bot's own drain with a recording socket at `paceMs` 3100) — on the merged v0.6.206 bytes 151d–e 0/2 and 153 1/3 (the green one is the chat-to-chat beat, unchanged by design). 2204 assertions (v0.6.206: 2199). Arena v1.4.8 unchanged.
- **v0.6.206 (17 Oct 2026)** — the round after R4, agreed with Johan on 16 Oct (21:27): the late bounce of R4's final smoke, the probe reservation measured before and after, and main's finding 1 (the chat beat) analysed; plus two faults in the actor's block windows found by the release's own smoke. The analysis and the proposals are `docs/specs/spec-next-2026-10-17.md`. *(1) THE LATE BOUNCE:* a bot target bounced a bot's bleed after the actor had resolved it (twice in ~470 bleeds through, each right after a Magic of the Smith). The fetch's note `fetches X from the library and equips V (…).` matched `L12.uEquip`: the actor's prey and predator opened a window for an equip that never was and said Pass, and that Pass reached the actor while its next bleed was still in its send queue — it answered the bleed's `Block?` before the prey had seen the bleed, and every answer after it fell one window late (the resolution even named the wrong seat). Three layers, each enough alone: the note reads `X comes from the library onto V (…).`; `_parseUndirected` opens no window for a BOT's line that names no minion on that bot's public board (a human's line untouched); `_askSeen` / `_staleAnswer` ignore a bot's answer that arrives while the line that opened my window is still in my send queue (`stale-answer` row; a human's answer is never second-guessed — the fx clone can show the action first). *(2) FOUND BY THE RELEASE'S OWN SMOKE:* (a) the guard's first cut waited for everything queued before the question; a modifier's add line is followed by my board push, so my prey's answer to the add line was taken for stale — 19 rows in the seven matches of the probe series, every one followed by the window running out its clock, one of them a bounce's new target's Block! (seed 9701: the Nosferatu took 2 pool it had blocked). It now anchors on `_openLine`, my last action line, never a note. On the final bytes it fired once in four matches, rightly: a hunt's line that my queue held for a second, while my prey was ousted by a Creeping Sabotage, opened a window in my new prey, whose Pass reached me while my next bleed's announce was still queued. (b) Older, since v0.6.144's bleed total: a `sleep(150)` between the `+0` line (and a modifier's add line) and opening the reactions-only window. A bot answers that line in ~50 ms at pace 0, so the answer found no ask open and was lost: the window ran out its clock (R4's smokes: 245 of 379 bleed-total windows, 14 of 97 modifier windows — first counted as 253 and 22, block contests that failed at the clock included; corrected with v0.6.207), and a bounce's new target's block attempt was never taken up — the bleed landed on the blocker. Both windows now open in the same tick as their line. Final smoke: 0 of 80 such windows ran out; the five slow ones were blocks taken up, their verdict at the clock. Both faults need an answer inside 150 ms — the arena, never a human table. *(3) THE PROBE RESERVATION (v0.6.143):* the minion phase set `this._probe` and reset it two lines later, so the probe ORDER worked and the RESERVATION never did. `_probeOrder(actors)` owns both; knob `probeHold`: 0 = as it played (the default), 1 = the reservation as designed, 2 = only when my actors out-number my prey's unlocked bodies (Johan's bait read); `probe-order` gains `hold`, `outnumber`, `reserved`, and `probe-hold` is new. Measured in the probe lab (`lab-probe.js`: 360 random tables, the same table in every arm, one whole minion phase against real bots; the order fired on 207): when my actors out-number my prey's unlocked bodies the pool landed per probe turn goes 1.92 → 2.73 (+0.80 ± 0.14, better on 43 tables of 91, worse on 5 — a prey with one body and a wake), the Govern landing 56 times instead of 8; when they do not, every bleed is blocked in every arm (0.00 → 0.00) and arm 1 holds the card for nothing. Arms 1 and 2 land the same; arm 2 holds only where it pays. In the arena the order fires about once a match (seven matches: reserved 3 of 3 at 1, 2 of 2 at 2). Default left at 0; the proposal to Johan is 2. *(4) Main's finding 1 (REPLY v0.6.204 §4.1), analysed, not built:* the chat beat after a question delays every message (4.96 s at the client's pace); measured on the bot's own drain, main's first idea (the beat separates two chat bubbles and nothing else; a hold or resume line skips it) takes the verdict to 3.04 s, the button and a typed `+1` to 0.20 s, the hold line to 1.60 s — waits for Johan's "kör". Sections 150 (10), 151 (5), 152 (2); destructive on v0.6.205: 5/5, 0/5, 0/2; 150e 0/2 on the first cut, 152 0/2 on the anchor alone. Arena v1.4.8. Handoff `HANDOFF-to-main-2026-10-17-v0_6_206.md`.
- **v0.6.205 (16 Oct 2026)** — R4 of the 14 Oct play-test review (`docs/reviews/REVIEW-playtest-2026-10-14.md`) with the dodge, in one PR (Johan, 16 Oct: "Kan vi ta detta tillsammans med R4 som en PR?"); the analysis and proposal are `docs/specs/spec-r4-2026-10-16.md`. *(1) THE DODGE (Johan, 16 Oct 17:47 + 18:06; open question A answered 21:20 + 21:27, after the PR was up):* `decide('combat-strike')` weighed a dodge only against the survival case (a known strike that would empty or torpor me, an aggravated one, an unknown one at blood ≤ 1). Now a dodge answers what the opposing strike does to me (damage + steal; their crows land anyway) against what MY strike would deal this round. That number comes from `_strikePick(vamp, theirDodge)`, cut out of `_resolveCombatRound`'s strike choice and played by it — the steal, the strike card or weapon, or the fist (1 + strength + the crypt modifier; 0 at long range) — with no side effects. With ONE dodge in hand a known strike of 2 or more that is more than mine is dodged (`known-out`); with TWO or more (`_dodgeCopies`; Johan: one can be sacrificed) a known strike of 1 or more that does at least as much as mine (`known-two`) — when mine does more, I strike (`known-keep`; Johan: "Roundhouse first"); aggravated (`known-aggr`) and the survival rule (`known-survival`) as before. EXCEPT when my strike finishes their vampire while theirs does not finish mine (survival comes first): with two dodges a strike that takes them to 0 blood or into torpor, with one into torpor only (`known-finisher` / `unknown-finisher`; their blood is `_stealAvail()`, unknown → no exception; my damage that lands either way — Carrion Crows, a borne Murder of Crows — does not count, the strike must make the difference). One dodge, torpor only, was my recommendation; Johan took it for now with a note for the META LAYER: what emptying or torporing a vampire is worth depends on the table position, not only on the exchange. And a dodge their crows make useless (their environmental damage alone torpors me) is kept (`known-wasted`). An unknown strike (I strike first) is read on the expected strike Majesty already uses (`_expOppStrike`, shared with `_ceCtx`: the table model's `_expStrikeDmg`, 0 at long range against a seat that has shown no ranged strike): `unknown-out` / `unknown-two` / `unknown-finisher` on the same rule; with `combatEndStats` 0 there is no expected number and the old blood ≤ 1 stands. Knob `dodgeOut` (default 1; 0 = v0.6.204's rule byte for byte), in the manifest's knob list. The knowledge-layer step (a deck that never hits for more than 1: dodge at 1 with one dodge too) is not built. *(2) F21, Revelations against a bot:* the human-line branch armed [aus] only on `… Revelations [aus]. Target: <me>'s pool.`; a vampire aim, no aim and a bare `plays Revelations.` (no mode chooser) armed nothing, and after the reveal the human's pick (the client's private `⋖ NAME targets CARD (revealed to you — they are targeting it).`, v2.6.159+) was dropped. Revelations is prey-locked by its text, so it now arms when the actor's prey is me, whatever the line's suffix (a bare play line is [aus]). After the reveal to a HUMAN actor the bot says how to pick (`NAME, pick the card I discard (Revelations): ⌖ target it in the hand I revealed to you, or type "discard CARD".`) and reads the pick from that actor's targeting `logTo` or a typed `discard CARD` (any case or punctuation, `_revCardByName`; bare or addressed `@Name discard CARD`, the bleed grammar's address), once, until the turn ends (`_revPick`); the card goes to the ash heap and the bot draws (`_revDiscard`, one body with the bot actor's `Revelations: discard X.`). Not a wait: the human's own action goes on. *(3) F23, the six uncurated V5 masters,* curated 99 → 105 (85 lib + 20 crypt): **Elder Library** and **Ventrue Headquarters** keep the auto tier's right shapes (Ventrue HQ locks for votes in my own referendums only — a known limit); **The Labyrinth** — a lock for +1 stealth to a Nosferatu (`fx.lockStealth`, `lockStealthClan`): `_lockStealthFor` offers the unlocked card from my board to `_bestStealthFor` (spent first on a tie) and `_stealthStack`; `_spendStealthCard` locks it (`X locks The Labyrinth: +1 stealth.`; the tally net reads `locks` too); **Guardian Angel** — on my sturdiest ready vampire at play (`host: 'ready'`, `_hostReadyFor`: the highest capacity, then blood; no second copy), +1 intercept in a block of a bleed at me (`_hostedIntercept`, at every site that counts a blocker's intercept and at the planning sites that price the predator's bleeds), prevent 1 once each combat beside the equipment prevent (`_hostedPrevent`; `VAMP prevents 1 damage (Guardian Angel, once this combat).`), burned when its vampire goes to torpor (`burnOnHostTorpor` in the shared torpor hook); **Wasserschloss Anif** — the bank: in my master phase a ready Tremere that can spare it moves 1 blood to the card (`_considerBankIn`, `decide('bank-in')` on the Blood Doll drain's rule — `nobody-waits`, `danger`, `last-blood`, `hunting-anyway`, `nothing-better`, `keeps-action`, `reserve`, `spare`), only while an uncontrolled Tremere can take the counters; in my influence phase it locks and moves every counter to the uncontrolled Tremere nearest to rising that does not go over its capacity — filled exactly, it rises free in the same phase, since the payout runs before the influence loop and the loop's first look is the free rise (`_bankTarget`; `BOT locks Wasserschloss Anif, Austria to move N blood to an uncontrolled vampire.`); the auto tier's `stealth 1` is gone; **Haven Uncovered** (Johan's decision 5: not played until the bot can rush through it, then on an opponent's vampire) — played in my master phase only when a ready, unlocked rusher of mine would rush THIS turn through it: the rush doctrine's best target (never a second Haven on one host), AND the rush through the Haven is what `decide('action')` would pick for that rusher now (`_havenHostPick`: the minion loop's own ctx via `_actionCtxFor` with the Haven's rush in it, the hold-the-Govern weight via `_actionCtxAdjust(…, ahead)`, the scorer without its trace row); held otherwise. It is tabled on my board with the host recorded (`X plays Haven Uncovered — on OWNER's VAMP.`, read by every bot: `L12.havenM`); any of my minions may then rush its host through it (`_bestHavenRushFor`, preferred to a rush card on an equal or better target): a +1 stealth directed action, no card from hand, the host not locked, the rusher the acting minion (`VAMP enters combat with OWNER's HOST (Haven Uncovered).`, `L12.havenRush`); the target bot opens its rush window (any unlocked minion may block) and on its Pass defends without the lock (`X defends (rushed by Y, Haven Uncovered).`). *(4) Found on the way:* **Arcane Library's influence lock never fired in play** — `_considerLockIncome` was called for the unlock and master phases only (the unit test 19l-13 called it with 'influence'); `_playTurn` now calls it before `_influenceLoop()` (Wasserschloss's payout rides the same call); and **a vampire already at capacity in the uncontrolled region never rose — and stalled the influence phase for the rest of the game**: the planner picked it (a need of 0) and the loop broke, so from then on no transfer was spent and no vampire drawn (a probe on v0.6.204: Lauren 4/4 and Chrysanthemum 0/5 uncontrolled — Chrysanthemum rises, then four transfers a turn go unused); the grinder's stock fills a vampire exactly all the time — superior Govern the Unaligned (Tremere, Malkavian) and Enchant Kindred (Ventrue): the smokes rose four that way in three matches (and four more in the final smokes), so on v0.6.204 those three decks' influence stalled often (older numbers for them may carry it); Arcane Library and Wasserschloss can fill one too now. It rises free at the top of the loop (`_riseFromUnc`, one body with the transfer rise; the influence sentence says `NAME enters play at capacity`). *(5) Found by the release's own pre-flight smoke (seed 4005), fixed:* the first cut played Haven Uncovered on the rush chooser's target alone — the Haven went on Christine Boscacci, then its rusher equipped Raven Spy (permanents first, 98), and a plain bleed outscored the rush anyway (1.2 vs 1.1); the play now asks the action scorer (above). The minion loop's action ctx moved into `_actionCtxFor` (the same finders, the same keys) and `_decideAction`'s ledger / probe / hold corrections into `_actionCtxAdjust`. *(6) Found, NOT fixed (older than this release):* the probe-then-punch reservation (since v0.6.143) never takes effect — the minion phase sets `this._probe` and resets it to null two lines later, before the first actor, so a prober is never kept off the heavy card (117g sets `_probe` by hand); the ordering half (the puncher last) works. Fixing it changes the stealth-bleed play v0.6.143 was measured with: a proposal, not a rider — Johan (21:27) wants it measured before and after, and his read goes into it: with more minions than the opponent, baiting with 1-bleeds that threaten a Conditioning follow-up gives the Govern the best chance of an unblocked action. And, seen in the final smoke: a bot target can bounce a bot's bleed after the actor has resolved it — one window, two looks (the first read the amount before the modifier, or passed on a +0 total; the second, 0.3–0.55 s after the actor's `through`, played Deflection); the bleed lands on the new target (the ledger pairs it, nothing vanishes), but the new target's block attempt is not taken up — the actor has moved on. Twice in ~470 bleeds through across this round's seven smokes; the paths are untouched by this release; next round. *(7) The coverage tool* (`gen-deck-coverage.js` v1.1.38): a MASTER mode counts as consumed only through a key with a board / master consumer (`MASTER_KEYS`); the hand finders' keys (stealth, intercept, prevent, dodge, strike …) never certify a master — before, Guardian Angel, Haven Uncovered, The Labyrinth and Wasserschloss read FULL. Its second version site (the report header) had stood at v1.1.34 since v1.1.35; resynced. The four V5 starter reports in `docs/coverage/` regenerated (they were from 18 Sep): Malkavian FULL 71 · PARTIAL 4 · HANDLER 2, Nosferatu FULL 77, Tremere FULL 75 · INERT 1 (.44 Magnum) · PARTIAL 1 (Bowl of Convergence [AUS]), Ventrue FULL 77. *Tests:* sections 146 (20: the dodge — one and two dodges, Roundhouse first, the finisher exception with the crows and an unknown blood, a dodge the crows make useless, incl. a two-bot relay where the strike played is the one weighed), 147 (13: Revelations — the arming shapes, a non-prey actor, the pick by logTo and by chat, bare and addressed, a pick from another seat, the turn's end, the bot actor's line), 148 (23: The Labyrinth, Guardian Angel, Wasserschloss incl. the exact fill, a whole `_playTurn` with Arcane Library, the free rise), 149 (14: Haven Uncovered, incl. the smoke's case and a two-bot whole turn through the rush); destructive on v0.6.204: 146 4/16, 147 6/7, 148 4/19, 149 1/13 (no abort); the late ones are red on their own pre-change bytes (149's smoke case 13/1, 147's addressed discard 12/1, 148's exact fill 22/1, the new 146 on the PR's first head — the floor of 2 at a tie — 13/7). 143d runs with `dodgeOut: 0` (it pins the survival rule); 139c's Revelations line became a Bum's Rush (Revelations no longer reads its Target suffix); 121f reads `locks`. `test-cardfx.js` 271/0: every master in the four V5 decks is `src: 'hand'`, and the six shapes (265/6 on the old curated file). Handoff `HANDOFF-to-main-2026-10-16-v0_6_205.md`. *Consumed with this release:* main's client v2.6.162 (PR #23, their `REPLY-to-bot-2026-10-16-v0.6.204.md`, and its docs-only follow-up PR #24): the read-only `elysium-vtes-bord.html` / `ELYSIUM-PROTOCOL.md` copies (the canary 69/0 against them); - **v0.6.204 (16 Oct 2026)** — R3 proper of the 14 Oct play-test review (`docs/reviews/REVIEW-playtest-2026-10-14.md`), the second half of Johan's "Kör på!" of 16 Oct: time and order at a table with a human. *(1) F3 / F11, a clock starts when its question is on the wire:* every wait on a human armed when its question was queued; at the client's pace a 6 s combat step reached the table with 1.5 s left, and the block question put the queue on its clock as a 'settle' ("3s" announced, 8.2 s real). `_afterWire` / `_wireWait`: no wait arms while a line of the bot is queued (no deadline, `status()` until null, an answer still counts); `_drain` starts the waits hung on a line once it is sent; the settle is gone, the number a question announces is its clock, the wait rows and Adaptive's answer times count from the wire. *(2) F4, the hold lines:* `chat(msg, { hold })` puts Holding / Still holding / Resuming at the head of the queue, tagged with the hold state they describe; `_drain` drops a line the table has moved past. *(3) F10, the verdict wait:* when the bot blocks a HUMAN's action it says whose move it is (`X blocks (stealth A vs intercept B) — Anna, add stealth, or say Pass to resolve it (3s → the block succeeds)`) and waits on the tempo (kind `verdict`, rung `resolve-verdict`); on the clock, the actor's Pass / No / No reaction / It resolves or the Bot button it declares the verdict from the counter (the §12 line) and goes on as the table would (`_verdictAsTarget`: combat, or the window again); the actor's It resolves over a failed counter lands the action at once; the actor's counter move, a typed stealth and the bot's own second intercept restart the clock; a hold freezes it. *(4) F2, one spokesbot:* the archetype read from the lowest-seated live bot only, as a log note (`NAME (bot) reads X's deck as Y …`), held while a question, contest, combat or live counter is open; an ousted bot is no longer the spokesbot; the actor's notes that repeated its own Misdirection / Fame / Pentex play line are gone. *(5) F6:* against a bot actor at the client's pace a bot target's "Hold on…" waits for the actor's question. *(6) F12 / F13 levers:* (a) no second beat before `Phase: Unlock.`; (b) a phase line rides the fx beat; (c) a phased combat's round / dodge / combat-ends summaries on the log tier; (d) the "Anything before …?" steps wait 1× the slider, the maneuver, strike and press steps 2×; (e) not built (Johan). Section 145 (35 assertions; 4/31 on v0.6.203, no abort); 127n, 127z, 128e, 128i, 139g and 141g moved to the new rules. Handoff `HANDOFF-to-main-2026-10-16-v0_6_204.md`.
- **v0.6.203 (16 Oct 2026)** — the first step of R3, Johan's "Kör på!" of 16 Oct: the vanished bleed, the strike step between bots, Deep Song against a human, hygiene from R2's smokes — and the consumption of client v2.6.159–161 (Tools below). *(1) THE VANISHED BLEED* (READ-ME-FIRST-2026-10-16c §6, found by R2's arena smokes: 21 bleeds in 12 matches, 3 of them on v0.6.201): Deflection, Redirection, Telepathic Misdirection and My Enemy's Enemy say "Change the target of the bleed to … (that Methuselah can attempt to block)". A bounce played on the bleed total (the late bounce, v0.6.144) lands in the actor's reactions-only window, where v0.6.114 refused every Block!; the new target's pending stayed contested and it dropped the bleed on the actor's It resolves. Now `_bouncedTo(seat)` opens the bounce's new target's block step in whatever window of mine it lands — the refusal stays for every other seat, and for everyone after a modifier (`_blockStepFor`); the open ask follows the bleed (`a.target`: status, the roster hook, the tempo's who); the old target's intercept goes back to 0 on the counter (my stealth stays with the action); a human new target is told it may block, with the clock. My bleed's `through` emit moved from the first ask's 'no' to the resolution, carrying `final` (what lands) beside `n` (announced, as the tools read it) and `bounced`: emitted early it closed my block counter (`_emit` writes mode null) — a late blocker's verdict then read stealth 0 from a fresh counter — committed my parked cost, and told the ledgers a bleed went through that was later blocked. As the target: a BOT actor's It resolves over my live attempt lands the bleed (a bot declares the verdict of every attempt it takes up before it resolves — an attempt it never took up was refused, or came after the window closed); a human actor is still asked. A bot actor's modifier closes my block step (blocks are over; a human actor may be out of turn and keeps the old rule). *(2) THE STRIKE STEP IN THE RULEBOOK'S ORDER BETWEEN BOTS* (Johan, 16 Oct: "the bot should answer known strikes"; "the minions first choose their respective strikes (the acting minion first, then their opponent)" [6.4.3]): R2's handshake left the strike step simultaneous. Now the acting bot strikes first and the answering bot looks at that strike before it chooses (`_awaitOppStrikePeek`: the line stays pending, so the existing reads — combat ends first, `_ceCtx`'s known strike — see it, and the consuming wait after my own strike takes it at once): no strike card or steal into their dodge (the hands; the card stays), a dodge against the strike that would empty or torpor me (`decide('combat-strike')` reads the known strike — damage, steal, aggravated — instead of the assumed 1; crows land anyway and are left out of the dodge's worth), none against a strike for 0 or their combat ends. The acting bot's wait for the answer is at least the handshake's safety net (`_strikeWaitHs`: the other side now chooses after it, behind its beat), and after its own combat ends it waits for no strike (the answering bot strikes no more). *(3) DEEP SONG AGAINST A HUMAN:* "The target vampire is considered the acting minion during that combat" — R2 read it for the bot handshake only; the human phase walk asked in the turn seat's order, so a bot that rushed a human with Deep Song spoke first in every step. One rule now, `_combatIActing(myVamp)`, for the handshake, `_phaseStep`, `_phaseDamage`'s row and the press-reply window. *(4) Hygiene from R2's smokes:* the bot's own prevent line (`X plays CARD: prevents N damage.`) is typed with the vampire's name — the generic shape matched it first and read `X plays CARD:` as the vampire, so the strike-vs-taken invariant never subtracted an opponent's Hidden Strength (all 6 'strike-taken' flags of the R2 smokes); the burn-for-pool and rescue notes reach the pool ledger with the bot's real name in them (`NAME (bot)` — the one-word `\S+` never matched since v0.6.108's naming: the Wider View drift); an equipment's pool cost writes a pool event (`_doEquip`); `_range` starts at 'close'. *(5) Found by this release's own arena smoke* (the invariant's 'taken 2, my strike 1, crows 2'): a borne Murder of Crows and a played Carrion Crows are two damage sources and both land each round [the cards' rulings: each inflicts its damage during normal strike resolution] — the victim kept only the last announce (`_oppCarrionCrows` is an `opp-env` event; it carries the amount per source now, and a repeated announce replaces its own source). *Tests:* section 142 (16, one live table on the real server: a bleed of 2 at stealth 1, the target declines and bounces it on the total, the new target blocks with Enhanced Senses and the actor declares "Block: Stealth 1 vs Intercept 1 — block succeeds."; red on v0.6.202: 6/10), section 143 (11: an in-memory two-bot relay — a dodge first keeps the answerer's Roundhouse, a Roundhouse for 4 first is dodged at blood 3, a fist for 0 at long keeps the Dodge, the decision itself, Deep Song's phase walk; 1/10 on the pre-change bytes), section 144 (7; 1/5 before the hygiene, and 144e red before the crows fix). 141m (Majesty first) caught the acting bot waiting out a strike the answering bot no longer sends — fixed before release. *Gates* (from `bot/`, the final bytes): suite 2077/0 ×3, canary 69/0 (main's copy), HB1 11/11, hotseat 11/0, check-versions agrees after the root copy; test-cardfx not run (cardfx and the twd files unchanged); beside main's client v2.6.161 (a scratch worktree of origin/main with this branch's `bot/` and root bundle laid over it): the client suite 300/1 (the one red = the embed drift: Help ▸ Bots still embeds v0.6.201's guide; a re-embed closes it), the client's canary 69/0, check-versions agrees. *The smoke* (seed 4003, 3 matches, 4 Expert bots, pool 30, the final bytes): every bleed paired (55) and VANISHED 0; the handshake, range and press 112/0, 112/0, 86/0 with no safety-net wait; the invariants 0 flags (R2's final smoke: 4); the block audit clean (205 actions); dealt ≠ taken in 5 rows, all explained (4 Hidden Strength, 1 Carrion Crows). Arena v1.4.7 unchanged. DEPLOY CANDIDATE; handoff `HANDOFF-to-main-2026-10-16-v0_6_203.md`.
- **v0.6.202 (16 Oct 2026)** — R2 of the 14 Oct play-test review (`docs/reviews/REVIEW-playtest-2026-10-14.md`): combat between bots, holds in combat, Roundhouse, two steals at once, and the rest of the v0.6.140 audit (Johan, 16 Oct: combining fixes that belong together is fine). *(1) THE BOT-VS-BOT STEP HANDSHAKE (points 16, 17a, 7):* the rulebook gives the acting minion the first opportunity in every step, and maneuvers and presses answer one another. Between two bots the maneuver, before-strikes and press steps ran side by side: each bot decided at once and waited 200 ms for the other's line — a wait tuned at the arena's pace 0. At the client's pace the line came ~600 ms later, so an Immortal Grapple was ignored (Theft of Vitae through it) and a maneuver to long was missed (Roundhouse at long); a late line leaked into the next round; and a press step nobody pressed in sat out the whole askSecs (19 s at the client). Now, when my combat opponent is a bot seat (known; the actor's blocker by the attempt line's sender; or unknown at a table with no human), the acting minion speaks first in each of those three steps — its card line, or a frozen 'nothing' line on the log tier: `NAME does not maneuver.` / `NAME has nothing before strikes.` / `NAME does not press.` — and the other bot decides knowing it and answers the same way; the acting side waits for the answer. A press to continue is answered with a press to end or `does not press.`, in either direction; between bots a press to end cancels a press to continue whatever the combat-mind knob says (a rule). The defender never plays a second maneuver to long, and an inferior Grapple on top of the opponent's adds nothing (a superior one still buys its press and the close range). A bot whose vampire goes down says `does not press.` once, so a waiting bot's press step ends. The waits' safety net is askSecs (never shorter than two paced lines), a `_heldTimeout` frozen while a human holds; every combat line wait shares one waiter (`_waitOn`; a replaced waiter resolves null instead of hanging). A line from an earlier round is dropped at the start of round 2+ (`_hsDropStale`: older than the last opponent line consumed); the Grapple stash is an event (`pre-strike`) like the press and the maneuver. Event kinds: `maneuver` / `pre-strike` / `press` with `none: true`. Round rows carry `hs` (a / b) and `hsMiss`; `ana-combat2.py` reports range and press-outcome agreement and the misses. Humans, and an unknown opponent at a human table, keep the old flow. *(2) Pacing (point 7, Johan's decision 2):* with a human seated and paceMs > 0, each of my lines in a bot-vs-bot combat waits a beat — a play the step beat, a 'nothing' line the short fx beat (0.4×); the arena (paceMs 0, no human) pays nothing. *(3) A hold stops my own combat moves (point 5, Johan's decision 7):* `_holdGate` at the top of `_phaseStep` / `_phaseDamage`, `_combatGate` before my strike, press, press to end, maneuver, Grapple and Carrion Crows. A step that would open under a live hold now waits in front of it (137f updated: v0.6.198 opened it and froze only its clock). *(4) Point 17b:* the tally's `rng` correction, the `rl` range lock, the `ce` combat-ends and the `pr` press fields count only from the human I fight (`_phaseOppPlayer()`); a bystander's auto-join wrote the counter's default range and a bot struck with its hands at long. *(5) Point 18, Roundhouse:* cardfx v1.9.2 writes `handBonus` for "hand strike at +N damage" / "this strike is at +N damage" (35 cards — `strike` is the ABSOLUTE field) and `_modeStrikeDmg` adds it to the vampire's hand damage (1 + strength + the crypt's modifier): Roundhouse [pot] with a plain vampire 3, with Preternatural Strength 4; Wauneka's [POT] 4. Rush planning reads the same helper, and the rush score no longer adds strength to a card's absolute damage. `gen-deck-coverage.js` v1.1.37 knows the key; `FXC_VERSION` 1.9.2 (v1.9.1 never bumped it). *(6) Point 19, two steals at once* [ANK 20200123] [LSJ 20041027]: the blood moves by the total of gain and loss and only the net is held to capacity (5/6 with Theft [THA] against Theft [THA]: 5, was 4); the round line says so. *(7) The v0.6.140 audit:* my last pool on my own Smiling Jack, an opponent's Jack and Fame's unlock drain oust me, and `_playTurn` stops after an ousting unlock phase (a pass, no master phase, no 'Turn passed.'). An opponent's Jack is paid from pool while comfortable, never my last pool while blood can pay, then blood across vampires, the rest from pool [ANK 20181006]; an empty vampire no longer lessens the obligation [LSJ 19971212]. *(8) Found by this release's own arena smokes:* a Deep Song rush at a bot with no other unlocked minion complied silently — the rusher's directed ask ran its whole clock while the target already fought, and the handshake's first wait gave up — it says Pass now; Deep Song [ANI] makes the target the acting minion, so the rushed vampire speaks first (`_combatActing` / `targetActs`; the counter's sides keep main's mapping) and the rusher arms at its announce for the target's early lines; a combat-ends strike is the protocol's one line (`NAME strikes: combat ends.`, then `Combat ends — CARD.`) — the dodge-shaped line was read as a dodge by a bot that resolved before the second line came; the press decision reads THIS round's taken line (`_rndAt`), and after the press step a downed opponent ends the combat (a Stray was burned for a press into a vampire already in torpor, then a phantom round 3); and, found by the second smoke (seed 4003), a range set for the NEXT round dies with its combat — a superior Immortal Grapple in a combat's last round set `_forceCloseNextRound` (the player) and `_nextRange` (its opponent, from `sets range to close next round.`) for a round that never came, and both bots' next combats skipped the maneuver step: one side never said its maneuver line, the other waited out the safety net and maneuvered to long alone (Roundhouse at close against Theft of Vitae at long); against a human the bot lost its maneuver in that round. The leak is old (v0.6.28 / v0.6.164); both flags and `_setRangeNow` now die in `_resolveCombat`'s finally, and the two next-round flags at `_clearCombatStashes()`. The same sweep: the round's range and Immortal Grapple's "hand strikes only" (`_handStrikeOnly`, reset only at the before-strikes step) outlived the round and the combat too — the next round's or next combat's maneuver step read the restriction (a sniping Tremere found no ranged card under it and stayed close), and the finders that plan between fights (a block's price, a chump bleed's cover, a rush's reach) saw "hand strikes only" (no Majesty, no dodge) or "long" (no fists) from the previous fight; 16 combats in the R2 smokes began after a final-round Grapple. The restriction now resets at each round's start, and the combat's end leaves `_handStrikeOnly` false and `_range` close. *Tests:* section 141 (36 assertions: an in-memory two-bot relay with a 300 ms delay for the races, a hold against a human, the pacing beats, the tally, Roundhouse, the steals, the audit, the event kinds, the rush, the combat-ends line, the fresh taken line, 141o — a two-bot duel ending on a superior Grapple, then the same Nosferatu's next combat against a fresh Tremere, and 141p — an inferior Grapple, then the Tremere that saw it sniping in its next fight); against v0.6.201 with its own cardfx: 32 red, 4 guards green on both, no abort; the five new ones are red on the pre-fix bytes too (31/5: no maneuver line, a miss on both sides, close against long, Roundhouse spent; the Tremere stayed close with Apportation in hand). The F18 value tests updated (Roundhouse 4 / Slam 3 / rush reach 8 / 107d / 127k); 137f updated to the new hold rule; 97d to the one-line combat ends; test-cardfx 265/0 (6 red against the v1.9.1 compiler). *Gates (final bytes):* suite 2043/0 ×3, canary 57/0, HB1 11/0, hotseat 10/0, test-cardfx 265/0, check-versions agrees; beside main's v2.6.160: client suite 298/1 (the embed drift), main's canary 69/0; the smokes: seed 4002 ×2 (7 misses, 4 bugs fixed), seed 4002 ×3 (no miss), seed 4003 ×2 (3 misses from the leak, fixed), the final bytes seed 4003 ×3 (integrity 112/0, range 112/0, press 86/0, no miss; `ana-inv.py` 4 flags, all older kinds — an opponent's Hidden Strength, Wider View's transfer burn). *Found, NOT fixed (older, since v0.6.144):* a bleed bounced late and then blocked by its new target vanishes — the actor refuses the block in its reactions-only window (the bounce cards say the new target may block) and the target drops the bleed as 'contested, unresolved'; 21 in 12 smoke matches (3 on v0.6.201). `ana-ledger.py` counts it now (a bleed `through` that no seat took).
- **v0.6.201 (16 Oct 2026)** — Johan's answers to the R1 uncertainties (16 Oct), a small round before R2. *(1) Debug, the whole table again:* v0.6.200 sent a debug bot's open hand seat by seat to the human players whenever another bot sat at the table, so spectators saw nothing. Johan: every human should see it, without patching around the base system — use the open-hand function as it is and let the bots never inspect a hand that is shown only by debug. Now one grant `openHand {to:'all'}` again (every human sees it, players and spectators, online and offline; the roster's watcher list is part of the audience, so a spectator who joins mid-game gets the hand at once), and every OTHER bot looks away from a seat that announced DEBUG mode (`_lookAway`; `_debugSeats` is keyed on the frozen announcement `NAME (bot) is in DEBUG mode` and its sender — an older bot's wording too; `NAME (bot) leaves DEBUG mode` ends it). Looking away = no hand intel (`_onOpenHandGrant` returns, `_handIntel` drops an open hand already stored) and no Revelations [aus] waiter answered by the grant (the target's private `revealHand` answers). The one exception is my own Revelations [AUS] on that seat while the card is in play (`_myRevAus`, set when the bot tables it); the view ends with the card. `_debugTargets` / `_openHandRetarget` / `_openHandTo` are gone; Revelations leaving play under debug no longer re-sends anything. *(2) A human's Reduce line:* `NAME reduces the bleed by N.` (a card name in brackets optional) from the bleed's target seat lowers the acting bot's total, never below 0 (`L12.reduceBy`); a bleed of 0 then goes through at 0 and moves no Edge, as in v0.6.200. It is the interim for every reduction card the bot does not know (Johan: a Reduce row on the client's Bleed menu — asked of main in `HANDOFF-to-main-2026-10-16-v0_6_201.md`). Pool-damage actions are not bleeds and are not reduced. Found while building: the first pattern also matched the bot's own reduce shape (`… — reduces the bleed by 2 (now 1).`, reading '(now 1)' as a card) and counted one reduction twice; the pattern excludes the em dash and '(now N)', and 140d guards it. *(3) Event kinds* `reduce` and `debug` (the event-log doctrine). *(4) Found by this release's own arena smoke (seed 4002):* a Creeping Sabotage tick that took Ventrue to 0 pool did not oust it — it drained its Blood Doll to 1 and played two more turns. The creep victim handler had no oust check; it ousts at once now (and the predator is sent the bounty). Two sibling sites of involuntary pool loss still have none (Smiling Jack paid from pool with no vampire, Fame's own-unlock drain) — the v0.6.140 audit, still owed. Guide: the debug passage, a Reduce sentence, a firmer target sentence. Suite section 140 (21 assertions incl. three LIVE — a late spectator among them; 13 red on v0.6.200, the 8 guards green on both, no abort; a mutant without the watcher read fails two); 139i rewritten to the whole-table grant.
- **v0.6.200 (16 Oct 2026)** — R1 of the 14 Oct play-test review (`docs/reviews/REVIEW-playtest-2026-10-14.md`): rules and deadlocks. *(1) Point 8, Turn-based deadlock:* a contested undirected block ended only when the HUMAN passed last; a bot passing last restarted a clock Turn-based never arms, and the table stood still. Whoever passes last now ends it (`_end: 'answer'`); the Turn-based reminder is read when it fires (`_remindArm` takes a function) and says nothing when nobody is left to wait for. *(2) Point 9:* the human's client writes its taken line when the table steps past Damage resolution — after the bot's ph 5. `_phaseNudge` took it as the answer to “Any presses?”. The taken and prevent lines now answer step 4 only, and restart no clock elsewhere. *(3) Point 1, the right to block a directed action:* the action memory (`_obs`) records the aim — the client's ` Target: NAME's X.` suffix, or for a card's own action row the aim of the play line just before it — and whether the action is directed (`_actDirected`: verbs, or the played mode's `dir`). Only the target of a directed action (else the actor's prey) asks what it costs or may block it (`_obsTarget`); for every undirected action, not only a referendum, a cross-table seat keeps quiet. *(4) Point 15, a reduced bleed:* the acting bot reads its target's frozen reduce line (`L12.reduce`, N4c's own shape) and lowers its total, so its `+0 (= N)` no longer undoes the reduction; the target that reduced counts the actor's `+N` on top of its own reduced amount (a human's or an older bot's `(= T)` still counts the bleed before it). A bleed of 0 is not successful: no Edge either way, and the resolve line says “The bleed goes through at 0”. Found in the review of this change: the target dropped its Edge on ANY pool loss resolved through the asked window — a human's referendum that cost the bot pool took its Edge; an undirected action never moves it now (`directed` rides on the asked pending). *(5) Point 20, the Edge:* taking it is said on the wire (`edgeTake`, `_takeEdge`), another seat's `edgeTake` clears mine, an `edgePass` to my seat gives it to me. Online the human's client clears its own Edge; offline the bridge drops `edgeTake` until main relays it (handoff). *(6) Point 22 and Johan's Tremere:* a card was “dead” when no READY vampire could use it, so a hand with no vampire out yet was dead from end to end and the first card went (Theft of Vitae). `_deadJudgeVamps()` — ready, torpor and uncontrolled, else the deck's crypt list — judges at all five call sites. *(7) The Creeping Sabotage count (the review's side find):* one tick reached the log as the predator's frozen line, its “ticks” note and (from older shapes) the victim's ack, and `_knownLosses` counted it more than once. The note is gone; `_evClassify` types `form` line / note / ack (all still `pool` events for the invariants) and the known losses count the line only. *(8) Point 19:* the steal is rules-correct (before mending; over capacity drains); the round line now says what drained (“steals 2 blood; 1 drains off at capacity 6”). *(9) Debug for the humans only (Johan, 16 Oct):* the open hand goes to the whole table while no other bot sits at it, else seat by seat to the humans (`_debugTargets`; the audience follows the roster; a seat that leaves it is revoked on its own, so the humans' view never blinks); Revelations [AUS] still opens it to every player; `--debug` / `--no-debug-weights` on the command line; the bot reads the room's helper policy (`joined`, `helperPolicy`) and refuses debug at a Tournament table, or leaves it when the table turns Tournament. *(10) Found by the R1 arena smoke (v0.6.200 vs v0.6.199, 2 + 2 matches, seed 4001):* Larissa's +1-bleed discard (VB) raised the action's amount but not the acting record made before it, so the v0.6.144 bleed total, the modifier decisions and the resolve line all carried the unboosted amount — a bot target took 1 on her announced 2. `ana-ledger.py` now tells a reduced bleed (`less, target reduced`) from an unexplained one (`LESS, UNEXPLAINED`). Section 139 (43 assertions, one of them live on the real server; 32 red on v0.6.199, the 11 guards green on both, no abort); test 127y reads the frozen creep line. No wire change beyond `edgeTake` (an existing verb). Handoff: `HANDOFF-to-main-2026-10-16-v0_6_200.md`.
- **v0.6.199 (15 Oct 2026)** — R0 of the 14 Oct play-test review (`docs/reviews/REVIEW-playtest-2026-10-14.md`; main's `REPLY-to-bot-2026-10-14-playtest.md`, PR #13), the groundwork. *(1) A PLACEMENT ALWAYS RETURNS* (main's Classic review, 13 Oct, §2 — the one hard stop): `slot()` clamps a Classic row's x at the felt's edge, so once its 4–11 distinct points were held `_openSlot`'s `for(;;)` never returned — a socket bot's process spun until the server dropped it, a lobby bot froze the host's whole tab. The scan stops after 200 indices and `_stackSlot` places the card: round the row's points in turn, fanned 10 / 12 px toward the table's middle per lap, kept on the felt; a freed point is taken first again. Structured has no clamp and is unchanged byte for byte. Section 138 (9 assertions; the Classic probes run in a child process, so v0.6.198 FAILS them after 8 s instead of hanging the suite — 7 red, the 2 Structured guards green on both). *(2) START-HERE-BOTS.html:* main's two sentences (§F — the human manages their own pool, blood, costs and Edge, Johan 14 Oct; a played card books nothing on the counter), the panel's Release hold frees only the bot on that row (main's 12 Oct note), 'curated to 100 %' corrected (99 curated; four V5 masters do nothing for the bot yet), the taken line is a report, the debug hand and ash heap explained (a tester's report, 15 Oct: tick Debug before Add bot, where the globe's menu is on a Structured table, the ash heap item, a debug hand reaches the other bots; the offline hand is a client fault — handoff), versions. One suite fixture (h17) moved its stealth tally into the action's turn: srv-2.6.42+ clears the tally at every turn. *(3) The read-only copies* are client v2.6.158 / srv-2.6.46 / protocol v2.6.158 (were v2.6.141 / srv-2.6.38 / v2.6.138): the suite and the arena spawn the live server now. *(4) Data:* third-party data comes from the source or, when the sandbox cannot reach it, from the private repo `dtagent2000/VTES-Elysium-data` (root + bot `CLAUDE.md`, README, SKILLS); `bot/.gitignore` keeps `twda.json` / `twda-vdb.json` and stray coverage reports out of git. No wire change.
- **v0.6.198 (12 Oct 2026)** — main's reply to v0.6.195–197 (`REPLY-to-bot-2026-10-11-v0.6.197.md`, revised 12 Oct) and Johan's principle, agreed with main: *a Hold on stops every bot clock that waits on a human, and the clock starts again from the beginning when the hold is released.* *(1) THE HOLD FREEZE:* `_holdHook`'s hold branch calls `_holdFreeze(who)`. The open block / reaction window (the holder joins `pausedBy` — a TYPED "hold on" now pauses it exactly as the quick phrase always did), my vote poll (the holder joins `rc.paused`), my pool question and an open combat or damage step each lose their timer, deadline and Turn-based reminder (`_freezeWait`). The arm functions (`_restartAskTimer`, `_pqArm`, `pw.arm`, `rc.arm`) re-check `_held()`, so activity during a hold restarts nothing, and the stopwatch does not ring on a held table. The release — `_holdHook`'s release branch, `step()`'s Release hold, `_releaseDeparted` when the set empties — calls `_holdThaw()`: every open wait re-arms FRESH. An answer still ends a wait at once (a line, Pass / No, Yes / No, a vote); completion is not a clock (a poll everybody has answered closes, hold or not). Main's table found the pool question (stage one answered No — and the bot said No block — while the human held) and the seven combat steps + the damage step (the step timed out, wrote the next `ph` and moved on) unprotected, and the Turn-based 2-minute reminder running under a hold. *(2) SAID FROM THE FREEZE:* a bot frozen inside its block window, a combat step or its vote poll says the hold itself ("⏸ Holding for X — say 'It resolves' when ready." + the 90 s reminder) — it never reaches `_holdGate`; a frozen pool question stays silent (every bot may hold one — debt 23c). *(3) FOUND ON THE WAY, and by an independent review of the diff:* a release by anyone ends every holder's own pause with the table hold (`_releasePauses` — the review found that a pause outliving the release left my poll with no clock and no button, and kept a target's Pass from answering my block window; a target's "It resolves" now lets the action through); a paused poll stays steppable; every freeze is said once — the block window, and a step or poll that opens under a live hold — and a question asked under a hold says so in its tail instead of promising a clock; `_releaseDeparted` frees my poll's pause and resets `_holdAnnounced` (the next hold went unannounced); a typed "it resolves" / "carry on" / "resume" answers a combat step as the quick phrase does; the late-bounce grace ("Any bleed modifiers?", 4 s) stands under a hold and starts again from the beginning (`_heldTimeout`, driven by freeze / thaw, cancelled with its pending); a new game thaws what the old game's hold froze; a pool question's Turn-based reminder dies with it; a held wait is not an Adaptive answer time, its `wait` row carries `held: 1`, and the mark resets with each new wait. *(4) BEHAVIOUR CHANGE, pinned in 96e:* a holder's vote (or "No") in my poll is an answer, not a release — the poll's clock stands while the table hold does; the holder's Pass releases it and the clock starts again from the beginning (It resolves still closes the poll at once). *(5) THE STEAL CAP READS THE HUMAN'S TAKEN LINE (main §3.2):* a human's client never applies combat damage to the board, so in round 2+ `_stealAvail()` takes their own taken line's `now M` (this combat — `_combatAt` — and this vampire) while it is newer than the board's last blood change (`rec.bloodAt`, stamped in `_scanOpp`); with no board record the line still gives the number. Bots are unaffected (their line and board agree). Suite 1859 → **1933** (section 137, 73 assertions; eighteen mutations of the new code each fail at least one, none by hanging; 96e rewritten for (4)). Canary 57/0 and hotseat 10/0 against client **v2.6.141**. No wire change, no knob (table timing and a rules read, not a decision). *Correction recorded:* the ▶ has been a filter since client v2.6.139 (`canStep && step && step !== 'release-hold'`); the 9 Oct ask was met before our 11 Oct handoff listed it as still open.
- **v0.6.197 (11 Oct 2026)** — Johan: "kör på din rekommendation" (the referendum line) "ska vi ta det tillsammans med nästa steg?" — yes. *(1) MY OWN REFERENDUM'S SWING IS SPOKEN* (the fourth silent pool change; the other three — a political action's pool cost, an ally's pool cost, a blocked action's returned pool cost — cannot happen with the four starters): after "The referendum passes, F to A." the caller says what the voters already say, `I gain N pool from the referendum. Pool: X.` / `I lose N pool to the referendum. Pool: X.`. `send()` types the line into the event log, so the line IS the ledger record — the explicit `pool` event of v0.6.180 stays only for the 0-pool case (the oust speaks there). *(2) THE PRESS SHARE:* `twd-profile.py` adds a fifth share per card and clan, `press` (a press mode, or an ally that grants one — Underbridge Stray), APPENDED so readers of the first four are untouched (rebuilt from the same sources: the old script first reproduced the deployed JSON byte for byte; the new one changes no value of the first four). `_twdExpect` returns `press` (null on an old four-share file); `_oppPressP(seat)` = 0.9 with a press-granting ally ready on that seat's board, else 1 − (1 − share)^hand. *(3) THE CALIBRATION* (Johan 28 Sep: the block doctrine and the wave already read the TWDA-fed `oppCombat` — calibration, not bricks). Read first, from 72 matches (1,842 blocks, 653 Malkavian combats): Swallowed by the Night as a maneuver 50 / as stealth 45, two thirds of the waves at 4+ blood against a hand strike for 1; a block with an intercept card weighed odds × urgency only, and the blocker's torpor by its blood at the react snapshot was 0 blood 75–95 %, 1 blood vs the Nosferatu starter 50 %, 2 blood 35 %, 4+ ~1 % (`REPORT-bench-controls.md` has the table). **`waveNarrow`** (`decide('wave')`, default **1**): a wave deck spends its maneuver only when the expected strike exceeds its blood (`torpor`), or when the strike is dangerous (≥ 2) and P(they press) < 0.5 (`no-press`); never against a seat that has shown a ranged strike (`ranged`); else `kept`. Knob 0 = v0.6.156, traced as `old-long` / `old-close`. Bench, Malkavian pinned (control = the no-knob arm read at Malkavian's seat): +1.36 ± 0.63 vs +1.53 ± 0.59, the rule fired 23 times, maneuvers 15 → 7 — no loss. **`blockCost`** (default **1**): a block with an intercept card — directed or undirected, a wake-raised body included (`_wakeRouteBlocker`) — weighs `_blockRisk`: the combat lands 85 % of the time as 1 + Poisson(λ), λ = 2.1 × (oppCombat − 0.29) (fitted to the 1,842 blocks; it reproduces the torpor table), cost = 0.5 × expected blood lost + 3 × P(torpor) (+ 0.35 for a wake), a usable combat-ends / dodge caps it at the card; let through (`combat-cost`) when the cost exceeds the action stopped + 0.5, unless desperate (pool damage at turns-to-oust ≤ 1 or lethal: `combat-desperate`). The first arm was INERT (0 vetoes in 48: the price never saw a locked blocker, and a hunt read desperate at low pool) — fixed and re-benched: −0.11 vs the control's +0.28, where the inert arm on the same seeds gave −0.09 vs +0.20; active vs inert paired −0.03 ± 0.43 — no loss; 19 blocks let through, the variant's torpors 41 → 36. Arena v1.4.7 whitelists both knobs. *Tests:* suite **1859/0** (sections 133 the referendum line, 134 the press share, 135 the wave, 136 the block price; 125e pinned to `waveNarrow: 0` — it tests v0.6.156's posture mechanics), canary 57/0, hotseat 10/0, test-cardfx 261/0. *Open leads (not fixed, `REPORT-bench-controls.md`):* the late steal line (1–4 % of steals), maneuver lines missed by the 200 ms window (range disagreements), `ana-block`'s R6 window.
- **v0.6.196 (10 Oct 2026)** — Johan's three answers. *(1) COMBAT ENDS BY THE OPPOSING STRIKE* (Johan: "build combat ends on expected / statistical damage — easier when the opponent acts"; debt 22c, his 18 Sep direction "save the Majesty for the dangerous one"). Knob `combatEndStats`, **default 1 after the bench**. `decide('combat-end')` weighs, in blood: what playing the card saves (the opposing damage and steal, capped at my blood) + 3 if it would torpor me + 1 when the [PRE] mode unlocks a locked vampire, minus the card's own blood (Majesty costs 1 — so a hand strike for 1 is never worth it without the unlock) and what my own strike would have done (half for a wave deck), against what the card is worth KEPT (0.6 × the table's most dangerous expected strike, shared by the copies in hand). The opposing strike is KNOWN when they declared first — the acting side declares first, so a human acting has already answered "What is your strike?" (Johan's point) — else EXPECTED: 1 + 2 × `oppCombat(seat)` (the TWDA-informed table model), 0 at long range with no ranged strike shown. Their aggravated strike (or a seat that has shown aggravated strikes) and a torpor always end it; their dodge never does. The weights (3 / 1 / 0.5 / 0.6) are judgment, not measurement. `_ceCtx`, `_expStrikeDmg`, `_combatEndCopies`; the reasoning rides the existing `decide` row (`ctx.stats`, `out.tag` = held / known / expected / torpor / aggravated / their-dodge). **The bench** (arena v1.4.6 whitelists the knob; `bench.sh ceV … knob=combatEndStats=1 ventrue` vs `negV … none ventrue`, 24 + 24 on the same seeds, design restored from the project's `design-balanced-serie-bal1.txt` — three lines re-checked against the old run logs): Ventrue's VP − table/4 **−0.11 ± 0.49** (6 wins) against the control's −0.12 ± 0.50 (5 wins) — no loss. **The knob fired:** 98 decisions in 20 of 24 matches — 68 held, 9 known, 14 expected, 7 torpor — where the control played Majesty 49 times. *(2) A STEAL AGAINST MY VAMPIRE WAITS FOR MY STRIKE* (found while building (1); a rules correction, unconditional): the victim side (v0.6.160) took the blood the moment the steal line arrived — before my own strike was chosen when they declared first (a human acting, every combat under the phases) — and gave nothing back when I then ended the combat or dodged, while the stealer correctly undid its gain (v0.6.172): blood vanished. `_stolenFromMe` parks a steal while my strike is unresolved; `_stealSettle` lands it at my resolution, or drops it after my combat ends / my dodge; a line arriving after my dodge / combat ends is ignored.
