- **v0.6.115 (16 Sep 2026)** — SELF-REVIEW OF THE STOPWATCH (Johan's standing "anything you're unsure of?" — two finds, both in the v0.6.114 timing work). *(1)* A `Hold on…` pause nulls the ask's timer AND deadline (line 4363); the v0.6.114 ring re-stamp read `!a.deadline` as "no deadline yet" and armed a clock on a window a human was holding — the window closed ~7 s later despite the hold. The ring never re-stamps a paused window now. *(2)* The stopwatch was armed only when a window OPENED (3 sites) while `_restartAskTimer` runs at every table activity (15 sites) and resets to the full `askSecs` — so one reaction played after the ring brought back the 20 s of dead air v0.6.114 had removed, in exactly the case that happens at a table. Silence restarts on activity, so every restart re-arms the stopwatch; a window now ends ≤ `reactClockMs` + `reactSecs` + grace after the LAST activity, whatever `askSecs` is. Section 89 +5 (89g; `diag-89.js` 27/0, 2 red on v0.6.114 — the two bugs). 964/0 ×3; canary 37/0 vs client v2.6.114; HB1 11/11.
- **v0.6.114 (16 Sep 2026)** — THE THREE TIERS + FOUR TABLE FIXES (Johan's pre-test round). *(1) The three tiers:* PROTOCOL §12.0 renders `log` without a bubble, `chat` as a calm bubble and `say` as the quick-phrase animation; the bot spoke on chat tier about nearly everything, so one card play reached the table three times (fx clone, frozen log line, and a bubble saying the same). New `note()` = the bot's system text (log tier; chat()'s persona rewrite; a leading "Trainbot: " dropped since the client prefixes `[name]`; named note because `status()` is the widget seam). Rule: an EVENT — play, tick, rise, pool change, turn banner, greeting, "Blocked. Combat begins.", the bleed resolve — is a note; a QUESTION or INSTRUCTION to a human — Block?, Any reaction?, Who blocks?, the pool question, Hold-on acks, "tell me if your side differs", the Revelations asks — stays chat. 86 chat sites → notes; 10 pure duplicates of an fx+log pair deleted (stealth/intercept/bounce/reduce/cancel-block plays, four combat step comments); 20 chats remain. Johan's calls: the influence lines are log (the `Phase: Influence` line already frames them), bleed-resolve is log for now — revert to chat if it feels too quiet. *(2) The acting minion:* `fxClone` takes `opts.actor`; the client already renders `[vampire] ➤ [card]` and captions "X plays Y" from `fx.actor`, which the bot had always sent as null. 32 minion-play sites pass their vampire; masters, events and rises pass none (the Methuselah plays those). *(3) Hidden influence:* a partial fill no longer flips the uncontrolled card face up (a v0.5 line did — "the vampire is now visible"); it stays face down with its blood counters public, as on a physical table, and the rise branch flips it — the rise fx is the table's flip. *(4) Reactions only:* after blocks were finally declined and the bot plays a bleed modifier, the re-ask is a reactions-only window (`_askBlock` `opts.reactionsOnly`): "Any reaction?" offering reduce/bounce/no reaction, and a `Block!` is refused out loud instead of contested — rulebook: a modifier never reopens blocks; the defender side had this since steg 5, the actor side leaked. *(5) The stopwatch re-stamps the window:* after the bot rings the room's Reaction stopwatch, the ask deadline becomes stopwatch-end + `reactGraceMs` (2000), never later than the deadline already held; `reactSecs` read from `joined`. Clock 5 s → the bot resumes 7 s after the ring (was the full askSecs, ~8 s of dead air after the countdown). Suite 937 → 959 (section 89, 22 assertions; `diag-89.js`; throws on v0.6.113 at the first note() call — a valid destructive proof, see SKILLS); 31 chat-reading fixtures retargeted to log tier (`.msg` → `.html`), one `waitFor` → `waitAny` (the bounce card-play line precedes the re-announce), three bot-vs-bot assertions now proven via the OPPONENT's inbound feed since the server never echoes a sender's own log lines. 959/0 ×3; canary 37/0 vs client v2.6.114; HB1 11/11.
- **v0.6.113 (16 Sep 2026)** — THE HELPER GATE. Johan's finding after the client's v2.6.111 helper-lock fix: the bots stopped narrating their phases the moment "All helpers on" actually held — and had narrated while the lock was broken. Root cause: `_announcePhase`, `_announceTallyA`, both `block-as-target` verdicts, the `Block!` handler and the clock's self-declared verdict were all gated on `caps.tally`, set only by OBSERVING a resolver counter on the wire, never by the helper policy (which the server sends on `joined` and as a broadcast, and the bot never read). The proxy was inverted — a helpers-OFF human gets the bare `free` counter from the first render and any ± click seeds the bot; a helpers-ON human must open Block/Vote/Combat first; offline (hotBridge) no tally ever arrives — and the same gate returned `classic table (no resolver) — let it through` for EVERY block decision, so bots at a helpers-on online table (and at every hotseat table, ever) never blocked until someone happened to open a counter. The arena hit this on 16 July and seeded a tally rather than fixing it. *The fix splits the two questions.* Game logic (do I block?) never reads helper state — both short-circuits removed. Structured narration — the `Phase:` lines, the bot's own stealth/intercept on the shared counter, the §12 verdict — is the bot's OWN protocol (a human saying "Unlock" aloud so the table can `Hold on…` and play into the phase) and is unconditional, same class as `Turn passed.`. A shouted `Block!` always opens the referendum (the old fallthrough conceded it unread; online the FIRST block of every game hit that path, since `avrOpen` sends the say synchronously but debounces its tally 120 ms). `_writeTally` mirrors its own writes into `this.tally` with the server's merge rule, so the clock's verdict works where no echo exists. `caps.tally` is diagnostic only. No policy reading, deliberately — the alternative is written up in `elysium-bot-helper-gate-decision.md`. Suite 921 → 937 (section 88, 16 assertions; `diag-88.js`; destructively 7 red on v0.6.112); one section-1 fixture retargeted from `waitFor` to `waitAny` (the rise's board push now drains before the `Influence:` chat, and the cursor walk swallowed the next `turn`). 937/0 ×3; canary 37/0 vs client v2.6.114; HB1 11/11; seed-free 3-bot arena: 17 windows, 3 blocks weighed, 0 `classic table` verdicts. Handover: `HANDOFF-to-main-project-2026-09-16.md`.
- **v0.6.112 (15 Sep 2026)** — R4 OF JOHAN'S ONLINE FEEDBACK ROUND: THE DEBUG MODE (#3b — the item the 14 Sep handoff omitted entirely; Johan asked for it under his point 3 and the cataloguer kept only the "hide the influenced vampire" half). `o.debug` opens the bot's hand to the whole table, turns its uncontrolled **crypt** face up (crypt only — a face-down library permanent is not the bot's to expose), and writes the decision weighting to the log; `o.debugWeights` mutes that last half alone, because hand + crypt is often enough and the weighting is chatty. **Off by default**, and never for a tournament: it is perfect information about one seat. **Armed on the deal, not at construction** — `openHand` is started-gated by the server, so a grant sent before the match exists is dropped silently — and **announced in chat**, because a table that can see the bot's hand must know it can; a silent debug mode is an unfair table, not a debug mode. *The live view (Johan's question, answered from the code):* the server stores nothing and only routes the granter's `cards` list, so a grant is a snapshot unless the granter re-pushes — but the receiving client overwrites its stored grant and re-renders immediately, so a re-push **is** a live update. The bot's hand has **34 mutation sites**; hooking each one is how you silently miss the 35th, and a debug view frozen on a stale hand is worse than none — Johan would grade decisions against the wrong cards without ever being told. So `_syncOpenHand` runs from the **one** board-push flush every one of those sites already ends in, behind a signature guard (order-sensitive: a reordered hand is a different view) so an unchanged hand never reaches the wire. This mirrors the client's own architecture exactly — `refreshOpenHands()` hangs off `schedulePush()` for the same reason. The weighting hangs off `decide()`, already the single funnel every judgement passes through, and is **status tier, never speech**: it is reference material to scroll back through, and at chat tier it would drown the table it exists to explain. Suite 901 → 921 (section 87, 20 assertions; destructively verified against v0.6.111 — 7 red there), 921/0 ×3; canary 37/0 vs client v2.6.107; HB1 11/11. Delivered alongside `HANDOFF-to-main-project-2026-09-15.md`: the toggle itself is a client control.
- **v0.6.111 (15 Sep 2026)** — R3 OF JOHAN'S ONLINE FEEDBACK ROUND: POLITICS. The client's Vote submenu (v2.6.106) reaches the bot. *Parsers:* `L12.political` (`NAME calls a political action.` + optional Target suffix) and `L12.votes` (`NAME casts N vote(s) for|against.`), both frozen §12 client shapes. *The block step (#10):* a political action IS an action, so it opens R2's pool question **by itself** (`o.politicsAsk`, default on; `false` restores R2's prompt-only behaviour) instead of waiting for an "Any blocks?" a table heading into a referendum rarely pauses to say. Everything downstream is R2's, untouched — the ladder, the T1 pending, the block decision, and the deduction at the actor's `It resolves`. That last part is Johan's requirement verbatim ("håller i den informationen tills dess att voteringen är klar och handlingen är Resolved, varpå den reducerar sin pool precis som med bleed") and needed **no new code at all**: the R2 pending already survives an arbitrary interval and deducts on resolve, so a whole referendum passes underneath it. *Title votes:* `_myTitleVotes` reads `title` + `votes` off the cardfx crypt entries — read, never inferred — counting locked titled minions (rulebook, and the client's own note on the menu item) and excluding torpor. `_castTitleVotes` fires once per referendum on the vote-mode edge, deliberately **before** the Protected District branch whose three abstain early-returns would otherwise swallow it, announces in the client's frozen shape so every peer parses the bot's votes exactly as it parses a human's, and puts the against-total on the shared tally (the P0 convention). *Direction is the R2 answer, not a new judgement:* costs me pool → vote against, costs me nothing → abstain. The bot cannot model table diplomacy yet, and inventing a preference it cannot defend is worse than silence — Johan's framing for the whole submenu was "some reaction at least", not a politics engine. Persona weighting, bargaining and vote-trading are the obvious next dimension, deliberately not v1. Every seat's votes are tracked as they land (`_refVotes`, cleared on the close edge) — the raw material a real politics engine will want. Suite 882 → 901 (section 86, 19 assertions; destructively verified against v0.6.110), 901/0 ×3; canary 37/0 vs client v2.6.107; HB1 11/11.
- **v0.6.110 (15 Sep 2026)** — R2 OF JOHAN'S ONLINE FEEDBACK ROUND: the table can now PROMPT the bot. *SAY 8/9:* `Any blocks?` / `Any reaction?` mirrored from client v2.6.106 (appended, never reordered). *The short-term action memory (#11, Johan's decision A):* `_onLog` remembers the last action-shaped line from another seat (`L12.actObs`: the client's action-menu family — bleeds for N, hunts, calls a political action, attempts to rescue, commits diablerie — plus a card play). Overwritten by the next action, cleared by the actor's `It resolves`, opens no window on its own; it exists so a later prompt knows WHICH action the table means. *The pool question (#11, decision B):* on a prompt with an unclassifiable action on record the bot asks "Will this action cause me to lose pool?" and on Yes "How much?" — quick phrases (5/6) or typed chat (yes/no/a number) — on the askSecs clock with "not blocking" as the silent default, because the bot must never freeze the table for its own uncertainty. **The answer collapses into existing machinery:** it becomes a T1 pending with `amount` = the number, so the directed `block-as-target` scorer weighs it exactly like a bleed of that size (`urgency = amount/pool`), `_reactWindow` says Hold on… and decides, `_commitBlock`/Pass follows, and `_applyPending` deducts the pool on the actor's `It resolves` — Johan's "reduce pool as with a bleed", for free. Bounce is barred for the new kind (an unknown action is not a bleed). A second prompt on the same action does not ask again; a prompt from a third seat is fine. Readable and steppable: `status()` has a `resolve-pq` rung and `step()` runs `_pqTimeout`, the clock's own body (the v0.6.109 rule). *The Who-blocks grace (#12):* after a human's `Block!` with no blocker line inside `o.whoBlocksGraceMs` (2500) the bot asks "Who blocks? Right-click the minion and choose Block." exactly once; a bot's Block! never arms it. The client's frozen `NAME blocks.` is parsed BESIDE `NAME attempts to block.` (both name the minion), and — Johan's "if the player runs the Block action first, treat it as the block" — a line that arrives BEFORE the shout runs the attempt handler itself, and the shout that follows is recognised as the same attempt (handler once, no question). Politics (`calls a political action.`) is R3 and reuses this ladder untouched. Suite 857 → 882 (section 85, 25 assertions; destructively verified against v0.6.109), 882/0 ×3; canary 37/0 vs client v2.6.107; HB1 11/11. Caught by the suite on the way: the first draft of the release note broke 82c's 4 kB line cap.
- **v0.6.109 (14 Sep 2026)** — JOHAN'S ONLINE FEEDBACK ROUND (R1): the seven items from `HANDOFF-to-bot-project-2026-09-14.md` that need no client round. *The widget seam (#1):* `status()` → `{state, waitingFor, until, seat, name, version, step, canStep}` and `step()`, two public methods and no wire change (bots live in the host's tab in both modes). `waitingFor` is the WHY in table language ("hold: Anna", "block? (Bob thinking)"), `step` names the rung so the widget can label its own button. **The frozen rule: `step()` never produces an outcome the bot could not reach alone** — the ask rung calls `_askTimeout()`, the timer's own body extracted verbatim for exactly this, so a CONTESTED window still declares its verdict from the tally instead of collapsing to "no" (a `_answer('no')` shortcut would have swallowed a winning block: the class of bug the rule exists to prevent). Holds are steppable and carry no deadline by design; a pacing beat (`_beatUntil`) and an incoming action's own reaction window (`pending.reactAt`) are READABLE with a countdown but deliberately NOT steppable. Single-flight falls out of the design: `_answer`/`_askTimeout` null `this._ask` first, and the hold rung clears its own set. *Timing (#13):* `o.reactClockMs` (7000, was a hardcoded 5000 since v0.3.10) is the table silence before the room's Reaction stopwatch rings — Johan's "the stopwatch starts too fast"; `o.reactPaceMs` (default 0.6 × paceMs) is how fast the bot answers a declared intention — his "the answer comes too long after". Deliberately two knobs, not one: the old `max(paceMs, thinkMs)` floor meant a persona could only ever make the answer slower. *#5 Vessel:* the bot always paid — `_bestMasterFor` reads `cost.pool`, the play site deducts, `push()` syncs, and the curated entry has carried `cost.pool 1` all along. What was missing is that nobody could SEE it (the play line says "plays Vessel on X." and a trifle's bonus action often hands the pool straight back in the same phase). An additive status line names the payment; the frozen play line is untouched, so no peer's parser moves. *#4 unlock:* the unlock already ran first — the bug was PUSH ordering, so "Gained 1 pool from the Edge" and "Vessel: 1 blood → pool" landed before the cards visibly turned upright. The unlock now pushes on its own, and `_applyPhaseIncome` `fxClone`s the card that caused the effect (Johan's "let the bot Target the card producing it"). *#7b:* the bleed-modifier chat is DELETED, not demoted — the two log lines above it already carried the announce in full, and per PROTOCOL §12.0 the question is the only speech the event needs. *#3a:* a partial influence fill no longer names the uncontrolled vampire; the rise line keeps it, since the card is public on the felt the moment it rises. *#8:* the greeting drops the version. **A v0.6.108 regression fixed, found by the suite:** `chat()`'s persona rewrite protected the combat handshake prefix with `.replace(/Trainbot combat/, name + ' combat')` FIRST — a no-op when the bot is literally named `Trainbot` (the default, i.e. every human table where nobody renames it) — so the second replace mangled it to `Trainbot (bot) combat`. Every receiving bot's `^(?:\S+ combat — )?(.+?) presses to` then captured `Trainbot (bot) combat — Lisa` as the vampire name: the bot-vs-bot combat handshake was broken at default settings since 12 Sep. The arena rotates names and never saw it; v0.6.108 shipped with the suite unrun (its own journal says so). A sentinel now makes the protection independent of the name. **Suite 831 → 857, 857/0 ×3** (section 84, 26 assertions; three section 16/18 observables retargeted from the deleted chat to the frozen `adds +N bleed (= M).` line; four combat-prefix matches in 20/36/38 retargeted to the persona name v0.6.108 put there; 60b rewritten to the planner spec's floor-as-a-stop (line 123) and 76a to the unified hold rule — both v0.6.108 doctrine the suite never caught up with). Canary 37/0 against client v2.6.107; `diag-hb1-smoke.js` 11/11. *Self-review before hand-off caught two of my own:* the ask deadline survived a `Hold on…` pause (`a.timer` was nulled, `a.deadline` was not), so the widget would have counted a paused window past zero and stopped — the stamp now dies with the timer and a paused ask reports `until: null` exactly like a hold; and the section-6 Reaction-timer test's 8000 ms ceiling had only 1 s of headroom once the stopwatch moved to 7000, the precise latency-flake shape section 5 already documents — widened to 12000.
- **v0.6.108 (12 Sep 2026)** — INFLUENCE + MULTI-BOT UX + THE UNIFIED HOLD RULE. *Influence:* transfers are a FRESH per-turn grant (`this.inf =`, not `+=`) with the rulebook's table-wide stagger (`tableTurn = (ownTurns-1)*nPlayers + seat`, capped at 4 — fixes seat 1 of a 2-player game getting 4 transfers on its second turn instead of 3); blood is invested INCREMENTALLY, one transfer at a time, and a vampire rises the moment it reaches capacity (the old atomic model saved transfers across turns, which the rulebook does not allow); `_planInfluence` simulates fill-orderings over the known transfer schedule and scores tempo (first rise turn) / efficiency (surplus transfers spent on crypt draws count as pipeline, not waste) / hand synergy. `_pickRaise` kept for test compatibility. Design: `elysium-bot-influence-planner-spec.md`. *Multi-bot UX:* only the lowest-seated bot (`_isSpokesBot`) greets or answers a bare `help`/`rush`; all chat carries the persona name with a `(bot)` suffix (the combat handshake prefix stays bare so the `\S+ combat` parsers still match); the damage grammar is scoped — a bare `bleed N` reaches only the sender's prey, `@Name bleed N` aims at one bot; `say()` creates a pacing footprint so the client's 3 s bubble plays out; a 30 s turn watchdog heartbeats only when the wait is the bot's own. *The unified hold rule (client handoff items 1/3/4):* `_holdHook` is tracking only — the acknowledgement and the reminder moved into `_pace`'s wait gate (`_holdGate`), so the bot that is ACTUALLY blocked announces, once, and arms the reminder. That removes the v0.6.107 relevance heuristic, which dropped the announcement (and the reminder with it) whenever a hold landed while no bot was acting. A release from any non-bot clears the whole name-keyed set (Bob can release Alice's hold at a hotseat — the client can drop its per-holder relay). The ask-window pause in `_onSay` finally applies the `players[].bot` filter `_holdHook` always had: the measured 2-bot round-3 deadlock (`pausedBy ['Bot2']`, `timer false`, `passed []`, permanent). **The client can now remove the v2.6.95 workaround that filters a bot's `say` index 0 out of the hotseat fan-out.** *Handoff item 2:* a `turn` arriving while `_acting` is queued in `_pendingTurn` and replayed from `_playTurn`'s `finally` behind a staleness guard (`turnSeat === seat`), which also covers the throw path. No `_pace` ceiling added — see the learnings note.
- **v0.6.107 (11 Sep 2026, B1 — the undirected block window)** — bots now get a REACTION WINDOW for the other bots' undirected actions (hunt, equip/employ, recruit, blood-stock, tabling, fetch, strength) and for Deep Song rushes against their vampires -- the L12.undirected grammar family parses the bot's own frozen announce lines, the actor's prey answers first and its predator second (the predator bot opens on the prey's Pass; a human prey's silence leaves the actor's askSecs as the ceiling), and the actor's Block? resolves when every eligible blocker has passed instead of at the timeout; decide('block-as-target') gains the doctrine's threat classes T2 intercept / T3 combat / T4 horde / T4b minions / T5 starve-rush / T6 tempo with the deck-axis multiplier, the posture-based combatFit and the lockCost (the A&B price of the block) -- the directed T1 bleed path is byte-identical, its axes come with B2; every window emits block-eval {T, role, score, parts} for the dossier and the arena counts blocks per class.
- **v0.6.106 (11 Sep 2026)** — A3 robustness (review #3/#5/#7/#11/#12/#14): the ask remembers its own target (the ghost-prey roster hook works for the first time); departed holders/thinkers (offline, vacant, out) release Hold on and ask pauses; the orphaned 10 kB note line archived; token reconnect with backoff (opt-in for injected transports, never on `close()`, no deck re-upload on rejoin, resumes an own turn that came while away); the blocker's vampire name rides the frozen log lines `NAME attempts to block.` / `NAME plays CARD: +N intercept.` (the 'Trainbot:' chat parse retired). Suite 789 → 806 (section 82). table.js v1.1.0 `--decks`.
- **v0.6.105 (11 Sep 2026)** — track C readers: `_bestBleedModifierFor` reads `req.targetPoolMax` (Foreshadowing Destruction's [DOM] +3 only vs ≤ 9 pool, fail-closed on unknown); Spying Mission no longer offered as a +2 modifier (curated); `_combatOppName` dies with its combat and at every own announce (a stale name outranked the next blocker in `_expectedOppVamp` — an A1 regression caught by the standing question). Suite 781 → 789 (section 81).
- **v0.6.104 (11 Sep 2026)** — A2, the sibling sweep (review #8/#6/#10/#13): the Smiling Jack victim side reads opponents' pubs from `this.table` (no bot ever paid for another player's Jack; fixtures 49d/62a had codified the roster shape); the chip-saver and the Jack floor read `this.persona` (object personas and `--knob` overrides now reach them); Blood Doll/Vessel never drain a vampire's last blood while pool > poolFloor; A1 riders: per-actor prey re-decide after the pace beat, `_settleOusts` prey-only. Suite 772 → 781 (section 80).
- **v0.6.103 (11 Sep 2026)** — A1, the deep-review NOW round (`elysium-bot-review-2026-09-11.md` #1/#2/#4): the combat handshake is opponent-scoped (the six opp-stashes cleared at every own announce, block commit and rush comply; `_stashAccept` name filter; bystanders never stash — proven 0/5 stale post-fix vs 2/5 pre-fix in a 3-bot arena); the press/maneuver/taken/crows/grapple parsers capture the bare vampire name; the minion phase re-decides bleed-target per actor and `_settleOust`/`_settleOusts` wait (bounded) for the roster after a possible oust; `interceptPotential` excludes attempted + Pentexed minions like `_pickBlocker`, and `_commitBlock` finds a route before saying Block!. Suite 753 → 772 (section 79), fixtures 37a/37l/37n/46e re-contextualised.
- **v0.6.102 (9 Sep 2026)** — HISTORY EXTRACTION + THE SPLIT: the 88 kB BOT_VERSION comment moved to the archive; no behaviour change. From here the bot is developed in the Elysium Bots project and consumed by the main project as a release (bundle: elysium-bot.js, elysium-cardfx.json, elysium-cardfx.js, START-HERE-BOTS.html).
- **v0.6.101 (9 Aug 2026)** — the late-bubble re-check in `_pace`, the drain-gap guard at the drain top, the Hold on tail guard.

## Tools
- **The v5 TWDA (10 Nov 2026, with bot v0.6.217; no bot release; PR #58, D-110)** — KRCG made v5 its default on 8 Nov:
  `data/vtes.json` and `data/twda.json` are the `data/v5/` files, the old ones frozen at `data/v4/`. The card pipeline was already on
  `data/v5/`. The TWD tools read the old list export and merged the v5-shaped dict by date + player + name, which counted a deck
  twice whenever the archive had corrected a name (69 decks on the 8 Nov files) and keyed the merged decks' cards by the article-last
  `_name` (the shipped profile's 14 dead keys). **`twda.py` v1.0.0** is now the one reader: the v5 TWDA keyed by deck id, names from
  `vtes.json` by KRCG id; a missing, non-JSON or list-shaped file stops with the fetch command (exit 2). Its `--self-test` (14 checks;
  10 mutants red) and `--check FILE` run in CI, the second on KRCG's current file, so the daily run now catches a TWDA drift too.
  **Output changes for the next rebuild:** `generated` is the archive's newest deck date (2026-10-25 on the 8 Nov file), no longer a
  hand-set build date; keys are sorted; decks are read in id order. `twd-archetypes.py` skips a crypt with no clan (a `None` key broke
  the sorted serialize) and reads the `deck-*.json` beside it (it read `/mnt/project`). **Verified in scratch:** the old archetypes
  tool reproduces the shipped file byte for byte; with 73 decks set aside (13 archive corrections, 1 deck the archive dropped, 59 crypts
  tied 6/6), the old and new profile tools agree on every card and clan; a rebuild from the full v5 TWDA gives 4,709 decks, 0 keys
  outside cardfx, shares moving by a median of 0.001, and 41 archetypes (Magnum falls to 9 decks). The rebuild itself waits (BOARD;
  O-37 the byte-for-byte rule, O-38 the 6/6 tie). Gates: test-cardfx 315/0, canary 69/0 ×2, doc-budget PASS; CI on the PR (the suite
  once 2393/0). Handoff to main: `krcg-audit.js`'s old side, the client's v3 fallback URL, `client/CLAUDE.md`'s fetch lines, the data
  repo's README. Egress: 12 findings (half-done 2, test-weakened 1, bug 2, unproven 2, stale-doc 3, rule-missed 2), 10 fixed, 2 listed
  (the bot reads the profile by the name it saw → BOARD; the 6/6 tie → O-38).
- **Round C4 — CI for `bot/` (6 Nov 2026, with bot v0.6.217; no bot release; PRs #54, stacked on #53, and #57)** — Johan's yes of 6 Nov
  to the clean-up spec's Q5 (D-109). **`.github/workflows/bot-gates.yml`**, beside main's `client-gates.yml` (which invites it) and
  with its conventions (read-only, every step even after a failure, one run per ref): on every PR and push to `main` that touches
  `bot/` or the bot bundle at the root it runs the gates that need no person and no previous bytes — `node --check` on every script,
  the correctness lint and (on a PR) a check that its suppressions only shrink, the root's bundle equal to `bot/`'s (a bundle change
  ships as a release), KRCG's v5 dump fetched and built into `vtes.json` (never committed), test-cardfx, `gen-precon.js --check`, the
  ledger's self-test, the canary against `bot/`'s copies and against the root's release, HB1, the hotseat gate, doc-budget and its
  destructive check, and the suite once. A gate that skips (exit 0 by design: the canaries, the hotseat gate, suite section 154f) fails
  its step. Still the round's: the suite ×3 (the release gate until C6), the sim footprint, the arena smoke and audits, a new section's
  destructive check, main's client suite when the wire changes (354/0 beside the bundle today, 1 m 44 s, but red by design until main
  re-embeds a new guide). #57, Johan's "best practice" (D-109): every PR reports the check `bot-gates` -- the job's first step reads
  the PR's merge commit against its base (`--no-renames`, `core.quotePath=false`, a here-string: a moved bot file, a Swedish file name
  and a long list all count), and on a PR that touches no bot path every gate step is skipped and the job succeeds, so the check can be
  required on `main`; the decision sits inside the job, so a run cancelled or failed while deciding never reports a pass; a daily run
  on `main` (03:17 UTC) catches drift from main's releases, KRCG's data and the runner image; main's files trigger no bot run on their
  own PRs (main's CI already runs the bot contract). **The correctness lint** (`tools/eslint.config.mjs`, ESLint 10.13.0 pinned, the
  spec's §5.2 26 rules, no style) covers every script in `bot/` but main's files (the server copy, main's canary), the generated
  wrappers and the ten legacy tests: the 25 files we own, and a new file by itself. Its first run finds 4 errors (a duplicate key in
  `gen-deck-coverage.js`; a duplicate key and two self-comparisons in the suite) and 22 unused assignments, suppressed in
  `tools/eslint-suppressions.json` until C2 fixes them; the file only shrinks. Proven: on a scratch copy a new undefined name, a new
  file, one more unused assignment and a grown suppression count fail, a fixed but unpruned finding fails (exit 2); the first commit's
  eleven steps ran green on a local fresh clone (the suite 2393/0); on GitHub PR #54's run 42221088419 was green on every step (the
  suite 2393/0 in 9 min) and the throwaway draft PR #55's run 42221112888 red on the lint alone (a planted undefined name; #55 closed,
  Johan deleted its branch). Egress: 12 findings, all fixed (the PR body lists them). #57's proof: the skip probe #56 (one root file;
  run 42229875164, `bot-gates` skipped) and #57's own runs, green; its egress, 7 findings, all fixed -- among them
  `test-doc-budget.sh`, whose cases passed on any FAIL line (two passed on the bootstrap's few spare bytes instead of their own check):
  each case now needs its own FAIL text, and a disabled path check turns two cases GREEN. No bot change.
- **Crypt round 0 — the ledger reads the crypt (6 Nov 2026, with bot v0.6.217; no bot release; PR #53)** — Johan's go of 6 Nov on
  Claude's recommendation (D-108: the crypt gets waves of its own, and D-98's "rows are playable" covers it). **`gen-card-ledger.js`
  v1.1.0**: every vampire of the same decks, read from KRCG's text (`vtes.json` beside the script; without it the page says the crypt
  is not read) — a row for each with an ability (the text after the sect and title, an effect class and kind per sentence, its curated
  keys and the crypt readers that read them, what is missing, its decks), the sect-and-title-only ones listed per deck, the crypt
  readers scanned from `elysium-bot.js` (each `_cryptFxOf` call's `fx.<key>` and its method: 14 keys) and the crypt class map (the
  sentences no key covers, by copies). Modelled = every curated key read by a crypt reader and every sentence covered by a key of its
  class (`FX_CLASS`; one key covers one sentence, the passive ones first); a partial row names the unread key or the uncovered
  sentence. The classifier is checked against all 20 curated crypt entries (20 of 20); a miss or a curated key with no class fails the
  run. The self-test (54 fixed checks: the extraction for every sect, every classifier rule a deck's row depends on, the readers' scan,
  the status; each one killed by a single-point mutation) runs before every page. The page regenerated from v0.6.217's runs (the
  240-match design and the V5 smoke, 14 × 12): the library half is the old page byte for byte but its version line; the crypt half
  (from `vtes.json`, cardfx and the bot's source, no sim): 186 vampires, 216 copies — 110 no ability, 6 modelled (the starters' curated
  ones), 70 unmodelled (81 copies), 0 unresolved; the biggest classes bleed passive 13 copies, combat activated 8, strength passive 8,
  action activated and intercept conditional 7 each. No bot change.
- **Round C3 — the mirror bench on the sim (3 Nov 2026, with bot v0.6.217; no bot release)** — `elysium-bot-sim.js` **v1.1.0**:
  `--bench none|persona=NAME|knob=k=v[+k=v]` puts the variant in one seat of every match and rotates it through every seat (24
  seatings × 4 = 96 for the starters), `--bench-deck DECK` pins it (bench.sh's 6th argument), `--knob k=v` is every seat's baseline

  read of VP and win share, per deck and for the variant seat; a pair needs the same seating, deal seed and baseline (the rest are
  counted, by reason), a `none` control is read at the arm's variant seat (it doubles as a pinned control), and an arm with no
  DIVERGED match is called inert. **`ana-bench.py`** reads sim directories. The controls re-validated

  `twdKnow=0`: +0.00, SE 0.03). The egress review: 5 findings (half-done 3, rule-missed 1, stale-doc 1), all fixed. Retired: bench.sh, run-bal.sh, poll-bal.sh, run-m.sh, run-t.sh,
  run-cmb.sh, run-ser.sh, make-bench-report.sh; CLAUDE.md's bench rules, HOWTO, README and ARCHITECTURE rewritten.

- **`elysium-bot-sim.js` v1.0.1 (19 Oct 2026, with bot v0.6.210)** — PLAYED AT 0 POOL: a bot that decides at pool ≤ 0 before its own `ousted` row is playing while ousted. The result row lists them (`zeroPool`: bot, round, question), `summary.json` sums them and `SUMMARY.md` prints the count in its header and the matches below the tables; listed, not excluded. - **The sim table (19 Oct 2026, with bot v0.6.209)** — **`elysium-bot-sim.js` v1.0.0** (new): the headless hotseat table under a VIRTUAL CLOCK. The arena waits, it does not compute (a 4-bot arena match: 212 s of wall clock, ~3 s of CPU), and the hotseat table has no network, so every wait in it is a timer: each match runs in its own process with setTimeout / clearTimeout / setInterval / Date replaced, and the clock jumps to the next timer when nothing else is left to run — about a second per match per worker. Same spec = the same trace byte for byte (fixed epoch, seeded deal). Seating: `--seatings all` (default, Johan 19 Oct: every possible seating, since it shapes the tactical choices — 24 for four decks, 120 for five), `random --matches N`, `fixed`; `--seeds K` deals per seating. `--profile client` (default: the client's hotseat spawn, paceMs 3100 / askSecs 20 / deckName) or `arena` (0 / 3). Knobs per seat, per deck or global; personas per deck. Arena-shaped traces + a `result` row (the arena's `computeVp` / `deriveWinner`; an oust after one Methuselah is left is a post-game oust, not counted), `summary.json` + `SUMMARY.md` per deck / per seat position / per prey / per predator. `--real` runs the same table on the real clock (the equivalence check: `docs/reports/REPORT-sim-table.md`). **`ana-sim-diff.py`** (new): two runs of the same design, match by match — IDENTICAL / SAME-PLAY / SAME-PLAY* (the same decisions per bot, rows interleaved differently) / DIVERGED with the first decision that differs. **`elysium-bot-hotseat.js` v1.3.0:** optional `personas`, `seatKnobs`, `onEvent` for the sim; `res.names`, `res.personas`; absent = v1.2.0 (the gate passes none — 11/0 on the new bytes).
- **`ana-windows.py` (17 Oct 2026, with bot v0.6.207)** — a slow reactions-only window (> 2.5 s from its line to the resolution) that saw a block attempt (another bot's `block-made` row inside it) is a CONTEST decided at the clock — split by outcome (blocked, or failed against the actor's stealth and went through) — and only a slow window with no attempt that went through counts as RAN OUT (an answer lost; the gate is 0). The first cut counted a failed contest as ran out: R4's smokes are 245 of 379 bleed-total windows and 14 of 97 modifier windows that lost their answer (first counted as 253 and 22); v0.6.206's final smoke 0 of 80 (5 contests, all blocked), v0.6.207's code 0 of 82 (5 contests blocked, 3 failed).
- **Tools (17 Oct 2026, with bot v0.6.206)** — **`lab-probe.js` v1.0.0** (new): the probe lab — N random tables where the probe order can fire (a Malkavian actor with 2–3 vampires, a heavy bleed in hand and at most one stealth card; a prey of 1–3 unlocked bodies from the four V5 decks; a third seat to take what is bounced), each played as one whole minion phase per arm against real bots, the same table in every arm (`node lab-probe.js N SEED ARMS`); each line carries its seed. **`ana-lab.py`** (new): per arm over the tables where the order fired — prey loss, bounced on, blocks, holds, the heavy bleed's fate, paired differences; split by out-numbering, prey bodies and prey deck; arm 2 derived from arms 0 / 1 (the lab is deterministic per table) and checked where it was run. **`ana-probe.py`** (new): the probe rows in arena traces — how often the order fired, was reserved and held a card, and what the turn landed. **`ana-windows.py`** (new): the actor's reactions-only windows (after a modifier, on the bleed total) — those that ran out their clock and went through (the gate: 0) against those blocked at the clock (by design), and the `stale-answer` rows.
- **Tools (16 Oct 2026, with bot v0.6.205)** — `gen-deck-coverage.js` **v1.1.38**: a MASTER mode counts as consumed only through a key with a board / master consumer (`MASTER_KEYS`: the lock-income, hunting-ground, permanent hand size, host income, neutralize, menace, Warsaw, Rebel, vote-lock and transfer readers, plus R4's `lockStealth`, `hostIntercept`, `hostPrevent`, `bankIn`, `bankOut`, `rushGrant`); the hand finders' keys (stealth, intercept, prevent, dodge, strike …) are type-filtered to modifiers, reactions and combat cards and never certify a master — they had made Guardian Angel, Haven Uncovered, The Labyrinth and Wasserschloss Anif FULL while the bot did nothing with them (the play-test review's F23). The CLASS map knows the new keys. The report header's version (the tool's second version site) had stood at v1.1.34 since v1.1.35: resynced. The four V5 starter reports in `docs/coverage/` regenerated (they dated from 18 Sep). `test-cardfx.js`: an R4 block — every master of the four V5 decks is `src: 'hand'`, and the six curated shapes; curated count 105 (271/0; 265/6 on the old curated file). The read-only `elysium-vtes-bord.html` / `ELYSIUM-PROTOCOL.md` are client v2.6.162's (main's PR #23, merged into the branch); the canary is 69/0 against them.
- **Tools (16 Oct 2026, with bot v0.6.203)** — the consumption of main's replies of 16 Oct (client v2.6.159, v2.6.160; v2.6.161 came with no reply). The read-only `elysium-vtes-bord.html` / `ELYSIUM-PROTOCOL.md` are client v2.6.161's (copied in; `elysium-server.js` and the hotseat plan were already identical). `test-bot-canary.js` = main's copy: their §5 runs the client's Reduce line (`BLEED_LINE`, extracted and executed) through our `L12.reduceBy` and a live `_onLog`, and pins the two DEBUG lines in PROTOCOL §12 — 69/0. **`elysium-bot-hotseat.js` v1.2.0** (main's REPLY v2.6.159 §6, run, not only read): the bridge's `tallyIn` calls `tallyCombatMerge` / `AVR_CMB_KEYS` (bare v2.6.129–158, `typeof`-guarded since v2.6.159), and our lift's context had neither — a bot's COMBAT tally threw in the lift and never reached the other bot (since v2.6.159: base fields only). The lift now cuts the three self-contained definitions beside the literal (`AVR_CMB0`, `AVR_CMB_KEYS`, `tallyCombatMerge`), so a combat tally relays its combat fields as at a real table; one context builder `bridgeIn(L)`; the result carries `combatMerge`. **`test-bot-hotseat.js` v1.2.0**: H11 pins it (a combat tally's fields, and a new round taking "close next round" and zeroing an absent `a`) — 11/0, the v1.1.0 lift 10/1. **`ana-ledger.py`** compares a bleed's `final` when the trace carries it (v0.6.203 emits `through` at the resolution). The guide (`START-HERE-BOTS.html`): the Reduce row named (client v2.6.159+) with the hotseat note (⌂ Home first; a Hold on gives the time), the offline Edge clear (v2.6.159), the eye in Structured (v2.6.160), the Debug box note (fixed in v2.6.159), client v2.6.161; and for v0.6.203 itself: a bounce onto a human may be blocked (the bot asks), Deep Song's rushed vampire is asked first, the strike step between bots in order. **`tools/run-section.sh`** (new): runs ONE self-contained section of `test-bot-logic.js` against any bot file — the suite's own preamble (`ok`, `sleep`, `mkClient`, `connectWithRetry`) cut from the suite at run time, a fresh server on a random port, a temp dir with that bot, the cardfx, the twd files and the decks beside it; the destructive check of a new section in under a minute instead of the ~300 s suite (sections 142–144 were checked against v0.6.202 that way). A section that calls a method the old bytes lack guards the call with `typeof`, so the old bytes fail clean instead of aborting.
- **ana-combat2.py + ana-ledger.py (16 Oct 2026, with bot v0.6.202)** — `ana-combat2.py` reports AGREEMENT (of the matched round pairs: the same range, the same press outcome), the step handshake (rows with `hs`, waits that ran into the safety net) and pairs rows on both crossed names (two fights of one vampire within 9 s were paired across combats). `ana-ledger.py` pairs the bleeds both ways: a bleed `through` of 1+ that no seat took within 8 s is `VANISHED`, with its cause — the late bounce whose new target blocked (refused by the actor) or a block attempt after blocks were declined; R2's smokes counted 21 in 12 matches (3 on v0.6.201), an open bug at that release.
- **elysium-bot-arena.js v1.3.2 (18 Sep 2026)** — the summary is keyed by persona/deck PAIR and a **per-deck standings table** (wins, VP, first out, dealt, taken, windows, blocks) prints below it and lands in `summary.json` as `byDeck`. With shark on every seat the deck is the axis B2 is measured on; no more hand joins.
- **elysium-bot-arena.js v1.3.1 (18 Sep 2026)** — SHARK for every seat by default (Johan: the bots must play correctly from their knowledge before novice/grinder deviations are measured). Bug found by it: with a one-entry `--personas`, random seating built a one-seat index and every bot fell back to deck[0] — the first all-shark series was five hacking decks (the referee's "7 distinct cards on 384 plays" and `seats 1..1` gave it away). Seating is now built over the seat count; the seats line prints the roster names (Shark, Shark-2, …) so it joins to the traces. Lesson: a detached arena does NOT survive a turn boundary in this sandbox — a series must finish inside one turn, or be split by seed.
- **gen-trace-harvest.js v1.2.1 (17 Sep 2026)** — "Who played what" section first: bot name → deck/seat per match from the manifest emit. Bot names follow persona ORDER and swap decks between matches; a per-name reading of b2s1/b2s2 had hacking winning 1 of 3 when it won 6 of 6.
- **elysium-bot-arena.js v1.3.0 (17 Sep 2026) — RANDOM SEATING BY DEFAULT (Johan: "5p30 ska vara slumpade säten i grund").** The v1.1 rotation moved every persona/deck pair one seat per match but kept its NEIGHBOURS — hacking preyed on sewer, malkavian bled into the tremere wall, in every match of b2s1 and b2s2 (which is why malkavian's 7 bleed-through per series measured geometry, not the deck). Now the pairs are seated in a random order per match from a run seed printed in the header; `--seed N` repeats a series exactly, `--fixed-seats` restores the rotation for matchup experiments (`--permute-decks` is a no-op alias). Each match line prints `seats 1..N: A/deck → B/deck → …` (predator → prey left to right) and `summary.json` carries `seating`. Smoke: 2×3-bot, seed 7, seating printed and carried.
- **gen-trace-harvest.js v1.2.0 + gen-batch-review.js v1.0.1 (17 Sep 2026) — THE PRICE REFEREE.** Johan's question after the 27 free cards: why did no 5p30 catch it? Because the arena has no referee — every bot reads the same cardfx and books its own pool, so a wrong price is paid by all alike and no metric moves. The harvest now takes the set of cards the bots actually played (play/through/blocked emits + master-play decides) and prices each from two sources that must agree: vtes.json (printed) and elysium-cardfx.json (paid). Reports played / priced right / **MISMATCH** / X-cost / unpriceable, and skips loudly when either file is missing (not a pass). Verified on a 3-bot tremere/malkavian/nosferatu trace: with Academic Hunting Ground's cost removed from cardfx → `MISMATCH 1: printed 2 pool, paid free`; with the shipped v1.6.14 file → 0. The batch scaffold gained an economy gate: read the referee before any strategy verdict; a mismatch voids the series. Scope (honest): it checks price DATA on played cards — a branch that carries the right cost and fails to deduct it is the suite's job (sections 90/91).
- **elysium-bot-table.js v1.1.0 (11 Sep 2026)** — `--decks a,b,c` rotates the registered ARCHETYPES over the seats (every live-table bot used to play DEFAULT_DECK); CLI main guarded, `parseArgs`/`resolveDeck` exported for the suite.

- **compile v1.9.4 + curated 105 → 115 (29 Oct 2026, bot v0.6.213)** — the wave's 10 readings (Villein, Quickness, Pursuit, Arms of

  Trelane); three cards that print it without 'on / during' (Eruption of Vitae, Growing Fury, Well-Aimed Car) are not tagged yet
  (no deck holds them; spec §4). `test-cardfx.js` 287/0 (+12).
- **compile v1.9.3 (19 Oct 2026, bot v0.6.211)** — A curated entry keyed by a printed name VARIANT is also written under the printed name.
  - The one case was *Pentex(TM) Subversion*: the printed *Pentex™ Subversion* kept the auto tier's empty entry, so a list typed with the printed name (KRCG's precons, the V5 precon generator) paid 2 pool for a card with no effect.
  - The variant key stays, so a list typed the old way still resolves exactly to itself. `fxLookup(name).name` equals the hand's own name either way; the master loop re-anchors by name.
  - Only that one lib key changes; crypt and alias are unchanged and curated stays 105.
  - The report lists the variant instead of "NOT in vtes.json". `test-cardfx.js` 275/0 (+4; 271/4 on the v1.9.2 compiler).
- **curated 99 → 105 (16 Oct 2026, bot v0.6.205; compile v1.9.2 unchanged)** — the six V5 masters the auto tier misread or that nothing consumed (the play-test review's F23; card texts checked in vtes.json): Elder Library (`handSize 1`, `handSizePermanent`) and Ventrue Headquarters (lock: `votes 3`) as the auto tier had them; The Labyrinth (lock: `lockStealth 1`, `lockStealthClan 'Nosferatu'` — the auto tier's `stealth 1` was read by no one); Guardian Angel (`host: 'ready'`, `burnOnHostTorpor`, `hostIntercept 1` with `hostInterceptOn 'bleed'`, `hostPrevent 1`, 2 pool); Wasserschloss Anif, Austria (master mode `bankIn 1`, influence lock `bankOut 'unc'`, both `bankClan 'Tremere'` — the auto tier said `stealth 1`); Haven Uncovered (`rushGrant 1`, the granted rush's +1 stealth — the auto tier said `stealth 1`). Recompiled with `node cardfx-compile.js --vtes vtes.json --curated cardfx-curated.json --out elysium-cardfx.json`: only these six entries differ, and the old curated file reproduces the deployed one byte for byte.

- **compile v1.7.0 (18 Sep 2026)** — REFERENDUM TERMS. Political entries get `ref: { tags, pool:[term], fx }`; every outcome key leaves `modes[].fx` (actStealth stays). One extractor, 14 cards with `ref.pool` (KRC, Parity Shift, Ancilla Empowerment + Anarchist Uprising, Autarkis Persecution, Camarilla's Iron Fist, Cold War, Consanguineous Boon, Conservative Agitation, Domain Challenge, Exclusion Principle, Perpetual Care, Political Stranglehold, Reckless Agitation), 7 tag-only. `req.titles` (which titles) additive on 14 non-political cards; `req.unmodelled` fails closed on requirements the data cannot verify. test-cardfx 231/0 (was 211). Coverage tool v1.1.31: `REF-TERMS` verdict + the bare-`unlock` false positive fixed (Freak Drive / Majesty were certified playable).
- **compile v1.6.14 (16 Sep 2026) — THE INHERITED COST (Johan's Academic Hunting Ground report: the bot played it without paying 2 pool).** The curated overlay is whole-entry replacement, and every curated entry that did not restate `cost` had silently lost the auto tier's printed price. Measured against vtes.json: **27 entries were free by accident** — 23 hunting grounds (pool 2), Papillon (3), Meditative Grove (2), Revelations and Magic of the Smith (blood 1). Rule: a curated entry without a `cost` key inherits the data's NUMERIC cost; an explicit curated cost always wins (`cost: {}` insists on free); `X` costs are never inherited (consumers do arithmetic on cost.blood). The report now prints three lists every compile: inherited (27), **mismatch** (Underbridge Stray: curated pool 1 vs data blood 1 — curated wins until Johan rules), X-cost without a model (Hidden Strength, Creeping Sabotage). Cards the data prices at 0 (Fame, Information Highway, Smiling Jack…) stay free. `test-cardfx.js` 212/0; json + js recompiled.
- **curated revision, 11 Sep 2026 (compile v1.6.13 unchanged; track C of the deep review, card texts verified in vtes.json):** Spying Mission curated — the auto tier's [OBF] `bleed:2` was a misread (printed: the bleed becomes unsuccessful, +2 banked for the next bleed vs the same Methuselah) → `{bleedNullify, bleedBank:2}`; Foreshadowing Destruction — the [DOM] +3 gated on `req.targetPoolMax 9` (bot v0.6.105 reads it); Blood Doll and Vessel two-way (`poolToBlood` mode; consumer = B2).
